# WoW Forever addon development reference

## Full review and authorized 1.0.16 release — November 14, 2026

The user requests an addon-wide review and authorizes CurseForge publication if the review passes. Reviewed all 92 runtime Lua files with syntax, static API/global/duplicate/dead-code scans and the full behavior suite. Removed three unused Mail helpers (ConfirmRemove, RemoveConfirmed and Categories) plus obsolete self.confirm state; current book.confirm and DeleteContact remain the sole Contacts confirmation/deletion path. Updated regression cases to exercise the active UI and shared deletion logic. No new concrete release blocker was identified. Core and matching TOCs advance to 1.0.16 with the pending tracking reminders and talent-badge colors. Native permissions, appearance, input and combat/taint smoke tests remain pending; the existing UseContainerItem taint investigation is not claimed fixed.

Final offline validation: node scripts/check-addon.cjs passes 92 Lua 5.1 files, identical TOCs/Core 1.0.16 and 46,381 substitute behavior assertions; all 11 separate gear-baseline tests and git diff --check pass. All 77 Lua test files are reachable from the test entry point. Static analysis against the pinned build-70862 documentation indexes 5,144 functions and finds no duplicate literal keys, unknown directly registered literal events or detected too-short documented calls. The two pre-existing, capability-guarded MerchantKnown pet-journal fallback APIs remain undocumented in that reference. Static and substitute checks do not prove native compatibility or taint safety.

[Review and publication record](release-1.0.16.md). Earlier no-release notes describe historical feature work and are superseded by this publication request. No deployment, junction or real SavedVariables changes.

## Talent comparison badge colors — November 13, 2026

Missing-rank +N badges use green for the active learning guide's NEXT talent and red for other planned talents. When no actionable GuideStep exists, including passive full-build/level views, fall back to the earliest unfilled point in the reference leveling order. Later spending, live mismatches and unavailable points must not skip an earlier missing talent. Color is informational and never overrides learning/purchase checks. Recompute from current native ranks; preserve LEVEL N milestone outlines, green OK and red X badges, click-through input and manual native learning. Passive coloring never starts the guide or spends points. Native appearance remains pending; no version or deployment changes.

## Permanent tracking reminders — November 13, 2026

Add learned castable tracking spells to the existing optional Missing Buffs bar, including Find Minerals, Find Herbs and Find Treasure when the client exposes them. Use the pinned build-70862 MinimapDocumentation.lua contract: C_Minimap.GetNumTrackingTypes / GetTrackingInfo return spellID, localized name and boolean active. Intersect native records with the guarded non-passive learned spellbook; exclude service filters and unavailable/unreadable states. Native tracking state establishes presence independently of aura visibility. No guessed spell list or automatic casts.

Tracking is self-only and explicitly out-of-combat-only, including when combat reminders are enabled; exclude it from native combat descriptors and legacy watches. Reuse Self upkeep (existing missingBuffClass preference), normal location/death suppression and secure manual spell clicks. Individual Track opt-outs use TRACKING:spellID and survive initialization before discovery. Native tracking events coalesce through the existing cancellable queue; existing resurrection events restore lost reminders. No polling, version/release/deployment/junction or real SavedVariables changes. Offline checks pass; native detection, rendering, persistence and taint remain pending in testing.md.

## Food refresh ownership pause identified — November 13, 2026

User confirms that one explicit Update immediately selected carried food. The ownership-pause recovery and carried-food selection are now native user-confirmed. Subsequent automatic refresh reliability, consumption, recurrence and running build/Interface remain unreported; the initiating ownership mismatch is still unexplained.

Native follow-up fails after the full food audit: user's level-45 screenshot shows usable Wild Hog Shank (841 health, required 25) while CM4 remains stale. `/wfs macrostatus` reports five item checks, latest BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED, and FS Food "Edited outside Suite. Automatic updates paused." This establishes that observation runs and the native-text ownership guard blocks the write; it does not establish who changed/restored the text. Read-only last-disk evidence agrees: account macroOwnership records food 4457 while macros-cache.txt records food 4599, both with combat potion 1710. Current in-memory bodies and the initiating mismatch remain unreported. FS Heroic Strike is separately paused and has a disk text difference; do not replace it as part of the food fix.

Use the existing deliberate Macros → FS Food → Update macro action, after closing Blizzard's Macros window, to replace the stale body with the current preview and record ownership again. This is a targeted recovery, not a reason to weaken external-edit protection or add another allowlist. Native resumption/consumption and whether the mismatch recurs remain pending. No runtime, deployment, junction or real SavedVariables edits.

## Complete Wowhead food candidate review — November 13, 2026

Review all seven live Wowhead Forever Food & Drinks listing pages (317 unique IDs; search-index excerpts still reported 322), then every individual tooltip. Review another 31 unique items from Pristine Peach's 153-entry shared-cooldown list, including edible raw fish, quest food and general Consumable entries. Record all 348 IDs, source URLs and disclosed recovery/XP/level facts in [the audit](research/forever-food-audit.json). These are website observations, not proof of native API categories or client availability. The source is discovery-dependent and can change; this is complete coverage of the reviewed listing, not an exhaustive future game database.

Ship MacroFoodData.lua before MacroItems.lua in both TOCs with 345 direct-use candidates: the 317 category entries plus 28 related items. Exclude deployable Grand Lobster Banquet/Specklefin Feast and equipped Skum's Bucket from direct recovery candidacy. IDs grant neither restoration nor XP priority: retain native requirements/use action and readable English tooltip checks. Listed category exceptions require seated eating/drinking and Consumable, Trade Goods or Quest class; ordinary food categories and the existing generic edible Cooking reagent detection remain supported. Include candidate exceptions in pending-data preservation and explicit pickers. Novelty items, alcohol and no-use items remain ineligible for automatic recovery without disclosed restoration.

Confirmed parser gaps: decimal mana amounts on teas/smoothies, Well Fed drinks excluded by the old buff rule, Scorpid Surprise's heals-damage wording and Restorative Bread's direct health/mana percentages. Correct these without reading player maximum health/power. Preserve explicit choices, food XP priority, current automatic-choice retention, native macro ownership/editor protection and combat deferral. Screenshot icons resemble Moon Harvest Pumpkin/Raw Black Truffle/Fine Aged Cheddar, whose reviewed health wording already worked; this audit does not establish the original live omission's cause. No release/version/deployment/junction or real SavedVariables changes. Offline and native follow-up are recorded in testing.md.

Own open-book values refresh snapshots only outside linked/guild/NPC/loading contexts. Unreadable values retain the previous snapshot. Unrecorded guides retain full references; unreadable live ranks are never masked by an older snapshot of that same open book.

Browsed guides use their own saved skill for material projections, Done filtering and current-step expansion, with an explicit Saved skill / last recorded status. These are last observations, not a claim of live data for closed books. Native recipe requests still require the matching open profession. ## Untrained leveling recipes — November 12, 2026

Live-learning follow-up: register NEW_RECIPE_LEARNED and TRADE_SKILL_DETAILS_UPDATE (cached 70862 TradeSkillUI event documentation and native schematic learning listener). Both use the existing cancellable zero-delay coalesced Queue/Discover/Render path. Re-read authoritative learned metadata and clear/reflow warning rows immediately on the next UI turn, without reload or reopening; a later details update handles metadata that was not ready at the learning notification. Ignore event payloads rather than assuming learning from an ID or persisting learned state. Preserve scroll/guide/step choices, combat/close/disable cleanup and optional automatic-selection guards. These two event subscriptions supersede the earlier no-new-events statement for this follow-up; no polling or new timer type. Native event timing/rendering/taint verification pending.

User follow-up requires all professions, superseding the original two-Blacksmithing-recipe fallback. Add ProfessionRecipeSources.lua after ProfessionGuideData in both identical TOCs: 122 explicit recipe-ID acquisition records cover every distinct recipe in the eight crafting guides, using 75 factual reference map locations. No broad profession-trainer default: acquisition kind and suitable rank-specific trainer pairs are explicit, with vendor/manual/quest exceptions. Cooking examples all use recipe vendors; oils/formulas, Alchemy protection/mana recipes, Engineering Thorium schematics, late Leatherworking/Tailoring patterns, First Aid manuals/doctors and Imperial Plate quests use their actual learning source categories. No unrelated route expansion.

RecipeSource prefers optional native GetRecipeSourceText and otherwise supplies catalog acquisition notes. RecipeLocation prefers a single valid native worldmap link, then uses reviewed faction-specific/neutral coordinates when native coordinates are missing or ambiguous. It never selects a native source list's arbitrary first faction. Uncataloged recipes can also supply a single valid native link; multiple/invalid links without a catalog fallback are rejected. Compact warning labels distinguish Trainer/Vendor/Manual/Quest while long details remain hover-only. Unknown faction never chooses a faction-bound trainer/vendor; neutral locations still work. Missing metadata does not imply untrained. Manual/automatic opening requires learned=true, and a completed request message replaces indefinite Opening recipe. Waypoint clicks retain visible/expanded/current-context/unlearned/combat checks and capability-checked native map/supertrack calls. No crafting, training, automatic navigation, added polling/events/settings, release/deployment/junction or live SavedVariables changes.

Explicit owner/target/view metadata covers nested hiding, size, lock, preview/reset and buff list/custom editors; talents now participate in ordinary indexing. Search opens the appropriate view and traverses owned anchor parents for scrolling/highlighting without invoking actions. Entire reveals defer during combat; page navigation/hide cancels them. User tab clicks wait for combat to end by staying inactive; previews stop when leaving their relevant views. ## GUI discoverability audit and navigation concept — November 5, 2026

User requests a deep GUI review because finding settings without search is difficult, with inspiration from easy-to-use interfaces beyond WoW. This pass produces a source-based audit and interactive navigation concept, not a replacement addon GUI. Existing uncommitted gameplay/GUI work is preserved. No game access, deployment, junction, version, feature-default or SavedVariables changes.

Current inventory: SettingsCatalog defines seven sidebar groups (six primary plus Setup utility) and 29 destinations. Static evaluation of GUI's literal page metadata and Catalog:Configure's initial page reshaping finds 111 basic rows, including actions/passive rows, before specialized forms. This is not a count of all individual controls. Current regenerated substitute layout models report a 500-unit viewport, with Bags & Bank content height 915, Action Bars 774, Gear Details 607 and Minimap 555. These are model geometry, not client measurements or usability timing.

Findings, ordered by impact:

1. Discoverability depends on knowing the category before seeing the destination. LayoutNavigation hides every inactive group's child pages; expanding one group moves all following groups vertically. Group selection returns its last page, which is efficient for repeat use but unpredictable for discovery. Existing workspace tests deliberately assert this design; those assertions must change with a redesign rather than constrain it indefinitely.
2. Labels predict overlapping destinations. Action Bars, Bars & Buttons and Castbars span different workspaces. Player buff borders live under Bars & Buttons, while missing-buff display choices live under Combat. Home's Maps & interface card opens only Minimap; the promised nearby window/chat tools are hidden behind the group switch. Professions currently means only Disenchant. Broad umbrella labels need descriptive child listings or more specific names.
3. Everyday controls are buried behind presentation labels. Hide in rested areas and Hide near a campfire require Missing Buffs -> Buff bar appearance, then an appended detail panel below the basic rows. Expiry settings appear after Position & Choices. Buff list/custom spell tabs are only visible after opening their manager. General two-column tiles give enable switches, related dependencies and secondary actions similar visual weight, fragmenting task order.
4. Tool workspaces and configuration are mixed. Home contains Characters, Leveling, Training Planner and Talent Builds; Everyday contains Macro Builder alongside merchant/mail automation. Opening a planner and enabling an integration should be distinct labeled actions. Browsing tools must never silently enable recording, apply a talent build or create a macro.
5. Search is useful but incomplete. Configure's basic-row indexing order omits the talents route; no talent extra compensates for it. Rested/campfire hiding lives in BuffSettings rather than indexed page rows, and the generic buffAppearance synonym entry does not provide individually labeled setting results. A redesign needs one explicit setting manifest feeding browse and search, including nested controls, instead of maintaining separate row/extra lists.
6. Implementation reinforces inconsistencies. GUI owns page data, navigation, row layout and many feature editors; sections use numeric row boundaries, and Configure repurposes rows by array position. Detail panels inherit the last row section's collapse state. Prefer stable feature/setting/section IDs, explicit section ownership, searchable reveal callbacks and shared control factories. Do not turn this into a broad gameplay-module rewrite.

Recommended direction: fixed sidebar destinations opening visible directories, concise labels and one-column related settings. Keep multiple independent features independently optional. Show normal controls immediately, with explicitly named secondary groups for uncommon choices; do not conceal whole features under a global Advanced mode. Keep search secondary and preserve its reveal-without-action semantics. Add direct related links for genuinely overlapping concepts instead of multiple independent copies of the same switch. A small Home directory should expose category contents and direct tool launchers; favorites/recent history can wait until the basic hierarchy succeeds.

Proposed complete route mapping (display names may change; route keys and slash compatibility remain):

| Destination | Existing route keys |
| --- | --- |
| Home | overview |
| Bags & Gear | bags, items, tooltips, professions |
| Combat | buffs, meters, actionbars, castbars, unitframes, combat |
| Maps & Interface | minimap, worldmap, windows, bars, chat, audio |
| Quests & Leveling | quests, leveling, camping |
| Vendors & Mail | vendor, mail |
| Tools | characters, training, talents, macros |
| Suite Settings | appearance, profiles, setup |

This deliberately has eight fixed destinations rather than seven expanding groups: predictable meaning/location matters more than minimizing the category count. Maps & Interface still needs explicit child descriptions, particularly for the mixed bars route. A later split into Progress Bars, Bag/Micro Buttons and Player Buff Appearance should retain old routing and separate feature keys; it is not implemented by the concept. Tool-related settings remain reachable from each tool as well as its directory, using one underlying setting.

Buff Reminders pilot: visible Reminders / Visibility / Appearance subnavigation; keep independent scope and combat toggles, place rested/campfire rules under Visibility with their out-of-combat limitation, name Buff choices & custom spells explicitly, and put icon size/lock/preview together under Appearance. The concept is a partial interaction prototype: it includes every destination and basic row, but dedicated editors, live game data, profile scope behavior, native movement and full advanced controls are not simulated. Sample values are local to the concept; they are not existing saved values or proposed new defaults.

Research consulted: [Things 3 feature design](https://culturedcode.com/things/features/) (hide optional detail while keeping the primary task clear), [Apple's 2017 Design Award winners](https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2017/06/apple-design-awards-celebrate-the-best-in-innovation-and-creativity/) (Things 3's award is external recognition, not proof this layout will succeed), [Linear's interface refresh](https://linear.app/now/behind-the-latest-design-refresh) (predictable action placement and quieter supporting chrome), [NN/g recognition and consistency heuristics](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/ten-usability-heuristics/), [information scent](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/information-scent/) and [progressive disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/progressive-disclosure/). Apply principles using original Suite styling; copy no third-party implementation, artwork or layout. Linear's own design rationale is a first-party case study, not independent user-testing evidence.

Validation: node scripts/check-addon.cjs --workspace-preview passes with 76 Lua 5.1 files, matching TOCs and 27,105 substitute assertions. This establishes the current baseline, not the redesign's usability or game compatibility. Concept interaction checks and task-based acceptance criteria are recorded in docs/testing.md. In-game visual/taint verification remains outside this audit.

User supplies a level-18 Warrior screenshot: draft Cruelty 5/5, four of nine points remaining, Follow talent rules on, and no second-row Fury allocation possible. Code review finds two over-restrictive paths that can cause this symptom. Capture spentAmountRequired when positive regardless of the native isGate flag, retaining authoritative currency/group scoping and legal-order simulation. A row spending condition need not be flagged as a displayed gate. Also accept a single ordinary minimum-level condition for a multi-rank talent; keep multiple rank thresholds and explicit partial rank caps unavailable until their rank semantics can be modeled. | [zTalentPlanner](https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons/ztalentplanner) | Forever, Classic, TBC | 2.6K | Direct Forever comparator: plan/save/compare in the talent window and class-specific website formats. |

Also reviewed the public [Talent Tree Viewer description](https://www.curseforge.com/wow/addons/talenttree-viewer) for editable preview integration. Do not assume Classic calculator URLs or Retail export strings encode Forever's changed trees.

Native staged changes are disclosed because the baseline is the shown native ranks, not a claimed committed snapshot. Saved build data is excluded from settings-profile rollback. Native footer mouse input is covered during preview; closing the popout or choosing live talents dismisses the overlay. All native parents, scripts, config state and buttons remain owned by Blizzard.

The calculator is explicitly **free planning with rank limits**: tier/prerequisite/point-budget validation, leveling order, website/native import formats, cross-class browsing without logging that class, and application to the game are outside this first iteration. The planner invokes no native purchase/refund/selection/staging/reset/load/commit API. No polling or OnUpdate: events and passive native post-hooks coalesce into one cancellable timer. Secure owned-root visibility driver hides input in combat; creation/layout/disable defers until safe, and disabling unregisters the driver to prevent later resurrection.

## Intermittent Overpower: event timing and cooldown rechecks — November 5, 2026

User reports Overpower still does not appear every time after the earlier cooldown correction; correct dodge direction, Battle Stance and sufficient rage were already confirmed. Treat the original implementation and previous candidate fix as insufficient for reliable live behavior. Researched primary addon sources: [SpellActivationOverlay action usability](https://github.com/ennvina/spellactivationoverlay/blob/main/variables/actionusable.lua) retries availability when cooldowns end; its [warrior module](https://github.com/ennvina/spellactivationoverlay/blob/main/classes/warrior.lua) excludes Forever from old combat-log-based stance-independent handlers. [ClassicSpellActivations](https://github.com/rgd87/ClassicSpellActivations/blob/master/ClassicSpellActivations.lua) uses older Classic event/glow machinery. [SpellActivationsForever](https://github.com/mwonng/SpellActivationsForever) documents server usability and optional UNIT_COMBAT windows, noting that target dodge events cannot identify whose attack was dodged. Review was architectural reference only; no addon code, assets, event-window inference or third-party dependency was copied into Suite. In particular, other players attacking the same target must not fabricate a Suite Overpower proc.

Suite now requests fresh public usability from player/target UNIT_COMBAT, player power/cast events, ACTION_USABLE_CHANGED and SPELL_UPDATE_USES as well as existing events. Unit combat payloads/miss outcomes/damage and action-change tables are not inspected. The initial coalesced 30ms refresh is followed by a finite 80/200/500/1500ms recheck burst; later activity may advance a distant retry but cannot postpone a pending near-term check. Readable standard-rate cooldown end times schedule one precise wakeup (+50ms), followed by the same bounded checks. These workers cancel at disable, combat/world exit and rebuild boundaries; there is no repeating idle worker or Lua OnUpdate. Availability remains a current readable server result, not a guessed five-second window. Unsupported/restricted timing uses event rechecks, never hidden-time arithmetic.

The public [SpellCooldownInfo declaration](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/forever/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SpellSharedDocumentation.lua) limits isOnGCD authority to SPELL_UPDATE_COOLDOWN. Capture that public classification synchronously and replace its snapshot on each cooldown event, before deferred painting; a readable inactive flag still takes precedence. Native highlight candidates now survive an initially false overlay query until GLOW_HIDE, allowing a later public query to establish visibility. This does not latch a false result into a visible popup.

Add `/wfs procstatus`: enable/preparation state, learned reactive IDs, latest public readiness reason, current icon-data availability and up to 24 readiness-reason changes from the latest fight. No raw restricted values, combat-log data, automatic chat spam or saved trace. History resets on the next combat start. Offline checks pass: 70 Lua 5.1 files, identical TOCs and 24,139 assertions, including late server updates without a second event, cooldown-end wakeups, secret GCD timing, unrelated/secret combat payloads, native highlight delays, no-legacy-skill classes and cancelled callbacks. ## Combat buff display research — November 4, 2026

User requested comparison with other addons, including a possible five-second icon check. Primary author documentation for [Opcow's Buff Reminder, Forever branch](https://github.com/opcow/BuffReminder/tree/forever) describes direct reads of permitted spells, Cooldown Manager frame inspection, prediction, and a Blizzard Auras display mode. Its Blizzard Auras mode places the native aura display over an always-present reminder; native rendering reveals the reminder when the buff disappears, without addon code reading the hidden presence. [Musca Auras' guide](https://github.com/dldvlpr-addons-wow/Musca-Auras/blob/main/GUIDE.md) likewise distinguishes native combat rendering from restricted Lua conditions. Its GPL-2.0 SecretAuraSingle.lua uses native aura containers/groups and display clipping for missing-aura presentation; no upstream code was copied. These are upstream approaches, not live tests of this client's Battle Shout.

The prior diagnosis correctly explains why Suite's current Lua-driven panel skips secret shouts, but an unconditional icon is not the only possible visual presentation: supported native rendering may provide a useful present/missing visual distinction without revealing hidden state to Lua. Recommend investigating a Suite-owned passive native aura display configured outside combat, covering all appropriate learned ranks and preserving native HUD ownership. Exact installed-build widget availability, permitted setup/cleanup, and refresh/removal/expiry behavior still require source validation and live testing before implementation. Do not inspect restricted textures/visibility, use pixels or Cooldown Manager frames to recover hidden state, or substitute cached/cast-based prediction for confirmed aura presence. Five-second polling cannot change API permissions and adds up to five seconds of delay; an engine-managed display avoids addon polling. Performance is unmeasured; local addon work affects frame time rather than ordinarily adding network round trips. No runtime changes, new timers or dependency installation were made for this research.

## Weapon hints and comparison preference — 0.2.29

The user reports broad casual operation looks okay on October 14, without thorough tests or a running build/Interface. Their only stated concern is missing weapon upgrade markers, previously deliberately excluded. They select an account-wide Stats / Item level preference. Keep Stats as the migration default; existing armor scoring, independent feature flags and preset selection remain intact. The searchable Prefer action in Gear Details cycles the two choices and refreshes existing overlays through Suite.Apply.

Scoring.WeaponScore defines a deliberately simple model, not a damage simulation: weighted bonuses plus base weapon DPS at disclosed preset weights (Balanced 1, Physical 7, Caster/Healer 0 for melee and 1 for ranged, Tank 1). Off-hand base DPS has a 0.5 factor; all equipped-hand bonus stats contribute once. Ranged/relic slot 18 is separate from melee. Weapon speed, ability scaling, procs, set bonuses, talent and hit modifiers are outside the model. Only validated public numeric ITEM_MOD_DAMAGE_PER_SECOND_SHORT data may supply a positive DPS contribution; nil/error/restricted data cannot become zero. The native API declares a LuaValueVariant stat table, not a guaranteed DPS key, so confirm that actual return in game before claiming native weapon scoring verified.

Build each candidate's valid resulting hand set against the complete equipped baseline. A two-hander removes both current hands, while its own stats and DPS count once. A single-hand replacement preserves only the actually equipped other hand; do not invent an inventory companion. Off-hand weapons need public CanDualWield=true and an existing one-handed main hand. Shields and held items cannot coexist with a two-hander. Reject unsupported two-handed dual wield and incompatible ranged/relic kinds. Item level uses a two-slot hand average (two-hander counts in both, empty=0), while ordinary armor/rings/trinkets use actual slot levels. Clearly label higher-level hints and best replacement hands in tooltips without implying stronger gameplay performance.

Contain usability/stat failures with pcall. Equipment reads retain a success flag so error/secret outcomes cannot masquerade as empty hands. Reuse GetInfo's native asynchronous item requests and existing item/equipment refresh work; no new frames, events, timers, polling, gameplay calls or globals. The only new persistent field is sanitized upgradePreference=stats|ilevel. Both TOCs and Core are 0.2.29.

Installed WowB.exe re-read October 14: **1.60.1.70374**. Cache exact ItemDocumentation/PlayerScriptDocumentation from pinned revision **735496de78ebd30ce816a0637982c556987f258c**. The complete 70338→70374 comparison changes only version.txt; it does not replace the older full-cache baseline. API declarations retain MayReturnNothing and AllowedWhenUntainted on item calls; pass only readable ordinary links, and public CanDualWield returns a boolean. Source/comparison hashes are recorded under weaponUpgradeHints in sources.json and forever-ui-source.json. **43 Lua 5.1 files, identical TOCs and 8,351 checks pass**, including fifty new behavioral assertions. Native API access/DPS shape, appearance, hand legality, taint and SavedVariables replay remain pending the focused smoke test.

## Compact nearby quest-item button — 0.2.26

Implement the selected quest-item recommendation with the Suite low-bag-space button's appearance rather than ZoidsTools' presentation: 48 × 48 slate surface, fine teal border/hover, 44 × 44 item icon and native cooldown swipe, LOW strata/level 1 below ordinary windows, no always-visible label. QuestItem.lua selects native quest-log special items with a readable carried-only C_Item.GetItemCount. Only incomplete quests, or completed quests whose native special item explicitly permits showing, qualify. Require known active quest blob or readable same-continent squared distance ≤ 250². Inside area outranks mere proximity; then prefer the supertracked quest, nearest objective and deterministic quest ID. Header/hidden/unknown identity/completion/item/count/location data cannot authorize a candidate. Native target range tints known out-of-range icons; guarded C_Item cooldown timing drives a native widget. No hardcoded quest/item catalog or broad zone fallback is added; unsupported supplied items and missing objective location data remain outside this first version.

PreClick outside combat rechecks exact selected quest/item and cursor state; a stale completion, changed item, disabled feature or drag release clears the action instead of retargeting during a click. No Lua item-use call or event-driven use occurs. A secure controller clears the action and hides button/parent for combat/death, then shows only the parent; fresh public out-of-combat work decides whether to show the button again. Protected creation, anchors, movement/drag interruption and attributes defer in combat. Disable cancels queued enable/work, unregisters events and clears/hides the action after combat.

Quest/bag/target/blob/cooldown/zone/world events coalesce through a cancellable 0.12-second timer. Public movement starts cancellable half-second proximity checks; stopping movement, unknown/zero public speed, combat, disable and logout end repeated work. There is no addon OnUpdate or stationary polling. Validate optional event availability and contain registration failures. Persistent additions are only an off-by-default feature flag and normalized position, sanitized with the existing common icon-position validator.

Never cache or replay hidden text; forbidden/unavailable observations invalidate the affected restore snapshot.

SetFont success is checked and invalid assets are protected with pcall; the preview falls back to a built-in font. The substitute hooksecurefunc now preserves native return tuples, including SetFont's success boolean. Native text color/range feedback, visibility, anchors, dimensions, cooldowns, protected parents/attributes and input remain owned by Blizzard. All construction, hook installation, discovery, font/shadow changes and font restoration defer during combat. Existing readable text-only native updates may be shortened in combat through SetText, whose declaration permits tainted callers. One controller observes bindings/addon/world/combat events and uses cancellable token-guarded discovery timers; disabled subscriptions/timers stop immediately. No idle OnUpdate, binding writes, extra keybind text layer or SavedVariable widget handles were introduced.

All **30 Lua 5.1 files**, matching versioned TOCs and **3,327 behavior checks** pass, including 99 new assertions for abbreviations/punctuation/gamepad/range preservation, independent defaults, restrictions, native updates/restoration, optional flags, failed fonts, partial APIs, event coalescing/stale work, combat handling and GUI/preview controls. ## Native settings close fix — October 10, 0.1.80

The user confirmed that the 0.1.79 native launcher opens Suite, but closing Options behind it also closes Suite. Their screenshot shows the 0.1.79 GUI; running build/Interface was not supplied. SettingsPanel:TransitionBackOpeningPanel calls ToggleGameMenu, whose native handlers reach CloseAllWindows/CloseSpecialWindows and hide the Suite GUI registered in UISpecialFrames. This is native close dispatch rather than the Suite GUI being parented to Options.

The launcher now calls GUI:OpenFromSettings. Outside combat, it closes Options with its native Close(true) method before opening the last-used Suite page, skipping the transition back to the game menu. It checks that Options actually closed before opening Suite. When HasUnappliedSettings is true, use the existing GAME_SETTINGS_CONFIRM_DISCARD dialog through StaticPopup_Show's supported custom OnHide callback argument. Native Apply/Discard/Cancel actions are unchanged. A cancellable next-turn timer opens Suite only if Options is closed after the dialog and synchronous menu cleanup finish; Cancel/Escape leaves Options open and ends the launch. The existing AfterCombat queue coalesces the complete close/open operation, including confirmation completion during combat.

Reviewed SettingsPanel close/commit and confirmation definitions, StaticPopup_Show/OnHide/OnClick, GameMenuEsc dispatch and UIParentPanelManager close/protected-function guards. These files are unchanged in the complete pinned 70245–70338 comparison; hashes and paths are in `.cache/settings-ui-source-70338/close-provenance.json` and [sources.json](sources.json). No native scripts, dialog definitions, UISpecialFrames entries, secure functions or panel registry were replaced. All **29 Lua 5.1 files**, matching TOCs and **3,228 behavior checks** pass. The 25 added assertions model native close cleanup, last-page retention, confirmation cancellation/completion, next-turn ordering and combat deferral. The 0.1.80 client smoke result is pending.

Mainline ActionButtonTemplate.xml supplies icon/IconMask, SlotArt/SlotBackground and Normal/Pushed/Highlight/Checked textures. One anonymous event controller and token-guarded 0.05-second discovery timers serve addon/world/show/art changes; no Suite OnUpdate is installed. ## Bag sorting on opening — October 10, 0.1.76

Installed WowB.exe was re-read as **1.60.1.70338**. Fetch and review [exact 70338 ContainerFrame.lua](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/943764493e6b16d63ded3ab304150d1f05e58b57/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Mainline/ContainerFrame.lua), cached in `.cache/bagsort-ui-source-70338`. The complete pinned 70245–70338 comparison lists this file as changed; six other recorded container/XML/callback/cursor/constants references are unchanged. This is a narrow bag-sorting review, not a full source refresh. Provenance, source hashes and complete comparison evidence are recorded in that cache and sources.json. Running GetBuildInfo()/Interface remains uncollected.

Matching ContainerFrame_OnShow/OnHide trigger EventRegistry's `ContainerFrame.OpenBag`/`ContainerFrame.CloseBag` with the frame, shared by numbered and combined bags. OnShow triggers before the rest of the native show handler and before its shown-bags cache is dirtied, so coalesce on a cancellable zero-delay timer instead of querying the native manager inside this callback or sorting synchronously. CallbackRegistry passes the registered owner before event arguments. Register and unregister only Suite's own callbacks; retain native scripts, parents, sorting button, input and window movement.

ContainerDocumentation declares no-argument `C_Container.SortBags()` with no return value or restriction marker. The unchanged native BagItemAutoSortButton XML and gamepad cleanup action call it directly. Call the service once, leaving bag flags, sort direction, filters and server sorting rules under native ownership. Do not infer a successful sort from a request. GameCursorDocumentation declares global CursorHasItem returning a nonnullable bool; missing, held or unreadable cursor state skips the current opening without ClearCursor or later retries. Ordinary-addon availability and native excluded-bag behavior still need a live check.

Constants.lua defines NUM_TOTAL_EQUIPPED_BAG_SLOTS as bag plus reagent-bag slots, preceding the bank bag offset. Guard readable finite integer limits and IDs before accepting numbered carried bags (backpack 0 through that limit); identify the native combined frame directly. Reject bank/keyring-only windows, forbidden frames and restricted IDs/visibility before comparisons or table keys. Seed existing shown numbered/combined frames only on activation, without scheduling a sort; changing another preference is idempotent. Later opens track a Lua-only visit until its last carried bag closes. No BAG_UPDATE subscription or idle updater exists, preventing sorting feedback loops.

BagSort.lua creates one optional anonymous event frame outside combat. A queued pass rechecks its token, visit, current option, visibility, cursor and combat state; mark the request before the native action to tolerate synchronous callbacks. Entering combat cancels the worker while retaining an unrequested open visit; PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED queues one pass only if it remains eligible. Close, world change and disable clear records/cancel work; disabling also removes Suite's callbacks and engine-event subscriptions. First activation in combat waits through the core queue and arms subsequent openings. No protected anchors, scale, movement, native frame creation or UI manager ownership changes.

The independent default-off switch is row six of **Windows**, with `/wfs bags` as a shortcut. All 28 Lua 5.1 files, matching TOCs and 3,034 behavior checks pass, including 96 added assertions for opening coalescing, numbered/reagent/combined bags, bank/keyring exclusion, repeated updates/settings, cancellation/stale callbacks/reentrancy, combat deferral, cursor handling, missing capabilities, restricted data, independent persistence and GUI wiring. Native action access, visible ordering/exclusions, window input, taint and saved persistence remain pending in testing.md.

## Quick loot — October 10, 0.1.75

Installed executable was re-read as **1.60.1.70338**. The complete pinned 70245–70338 comparison used by the preceding quest review lists changed Mainline LootFrame.lua (its calculated height gains a one-row minimum), so do not use the full cached file as an exact current reference. Five other relevant references are unchanged. Paths, SHA256 hashes and the inherited complete comparison are recorded in that cache's provenance.json and sources.json. This is a narrow review, not a full cache refresh; running GetBuildInfo()/Interface remains uncollected.

Matching LootDocumentation declares synchronous `LOOT_READY(autoloot: bool)`, `LOOT_OPENED(autoLoot: bool, isFromItem: bool)`, `LOOT_CLOSED` and `LOOT_BIND_CONFIRM(lootSlot: luaIndex)`. Use the ready event's effective automatic-loot boolean rather than writing or reconstructing autoLootDefault/modifier state. Bindings_Camelot.xml defines AUTOLOOTTOGGLE with Shift as its initial modifier; the player can configure it. Secret/missing/nontrue event values latch the current session manual. No raw flag comparison happens before Suite.IsReadable.

The exact native LootFrame uses GetNumLootItems to build its slot list, GetLootSlotType to choose item/currency/money elements and LootSlot(slotIndex) for native pickup. LootConstantsDocumentation declares None=0, Item=1, Money=2 and Currency=3. Suite's fast path capability-checks these legacy globals, bounds readable integer counts to 0–1000, rejects restricted/missing/unknown types and requests valid nonempty slots in reverse order immediately on LOOT_READY. Recheck the current count and active/current session/settings around slot queries and before requests; reverse traversal tolerates synchronous removal. These legacy function signatures are visible in native usage but lack generated declarations, so ordinary-addon availability must still be tested.

LootFrameDocumentation declares `C_LootFrame.TryAutoLoot()` with no arguments, no return value and no restriction marker; the native gamepad Loot all action calls it directly. Use it as a once-per-session fallback only when required slot functions are absent, without inventing a count or probing unreadable data. Native APIs retain ownership of bag capacity, locked/unique/group loot and bind prompts. No success receipt or runtime speed benchmark is inferred from the request.

`Loot.lua` keeps one optional anonymous event frame and a Lua-only visit record. Mark a pass before native requests because close/bind/ready events can be synchronous. Duplicate readiness never resubmits a pass, and binding confirmation pauses the remaining slots without calling ConfirmLootSlot. Close/world/disable clear state; no deferred loot actions, pacing/retry timer or idle updater exists. The event frame is created through the core combat queue; an existing frame can request ordinary auto-loot during combat without changing anchors, scale, scripts, visibility or parents. No native global replacement, CVar/auto-loot-toggle mutation, forced window closing, roll handling or master-loot assignment is added. Enable affects the next ready event, leaving already-open manual windows alone.

The independent Quick loot switch is the fourth row under the existing **Quests & loot** page, with `/wfs loot` as an alias; no additional sidebar tab is needed. All 27 Lua 5.1 files, identical TOCs and 2,938 behavior checks pass, with 70 new assertions covering settings/modifiers, synchronous timing/order, duplicate/empty/shrinking/unsupported/restricted data, cancellation/reentrancy, bind/manual cases, missing API fallback, combat construction/operations and GUI wiring. Native speed, context validity, ordinary-addon access, presentation, confirmations, taint and persistence remain pending in testing.md.

## Quest and gossip automation — October 10, 0.1.74

Installed WowB.exe VersionInfo now reports **1.60.1.70338**; running GetBuildInfo()/Interface remains uncollected. The matching [70338 commit](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/tree/943764493e6b16d63ded3ab304150d1f05e58b57) and complete pinned comparison against the full 70245 cache contain three commits and 208 changed files. The twelve listed quest/gossip/input/unit references are unchanged. This is a narrow source review, not a refresh or identity claim for the whole cache. Paths, hashes and comparison evidence are in `.cache/quest-ui-source-70338/provenance.json` and sources.json.

`GossipInfoDocumentation.lua` declares `GetActiveQuests`/`GetAvailableQuests` as lists of GossipQuestUIInfo, with questID, optional isComplete and nonnullable isIgnored. `SelectActiveQuest`/`SelectAvailableQuest` take quest IDs, while native `QuestFrame.lua`'s older greeting uses `GetActiveTitle`, `GetActiveQuestID` and the fifth `GetAvailableQuestInfo` return, selecting by index. Modern available offers skip ignored quests and include trivial/repeatable quests; complete active quests are selected first. Guard returned lists, rows and every used field. Sparse/restricted/malformed lists, unknown IDs and completion flags cannot trigger actions.

Gossip option records expose optional gossipOptionID, status and orderIndex. Use readable gossipOptionID with `C_GossipInfo.SelectOption`, never confuse it with a row/order index. Only advance exactly one Available option when both quest lists are empty and ForceGossip is explicitly false. Shared GossipFrame native code uses ForceGossip to preserve deliberate conversation display. No `confirmed` or text argument is supplied. `GOSSIP_CONFIRM`/`GOSSIP_ENTER_CODE` pause queued work and leave native prompts alone; these APIs do not expose all costs or consequences, so automation never accepts those prompts.

Matching `QuestFrame.lua` shows `QuestDetailAcceptButton_OnClick` checking QuestFlagsPVP, using AcknowledgeAutoAcceptQuest for QuestGetAutoAccept and AcceptQuest otherwise. Suite requires readable flags and leaves PvP/shared acceptance confirmations manual. It acts only on a shown native detail panel, leaving hidden item/area/adventure-map offers under native ownership. `QuestProgressCompleteButton_OnClick` calls CompleteQuest after the panel uses IsQuestCompletable. `QuestRewardCompleteButton_OnClick` maps one choice to index 1, zero to 0, and checks GetQuestMoneyToGet before requesting GetQuestReward. Suite leaves more than one choice and positive/unavailable costs manual. These legacy globals have native-source uses but no matching generated function declarations; capability checks and readable finite integer counts/IDs/costs are necessary, and source usage does not establish ordinary-addon availability.

Events are synchronous, so request deduplication and list-origin bookkeeping happen before native calls. One cancellable next-frame timer waits for Blizzard's show/transition handlers, then verifies the retained stage, shown panels, quest/NPC identity, current independent options, combat and Shift. Shift is latched for the conversation; repeated events cannot defeat a bypass or resubmit a request. Continuing GOSSIP_CLOSED retains the visit while cancelling the old stage, true closing clears it, and QUEST_FINISHED retains list-origin attempt history to avoid repeatable loops. After finish or a relevant PLAYER_INTERACTION_MANAGER_FRAME_HIDE, the same worker checks both Gossip and QuestGiver through C_PlayerInteractionManager.IsInteractingWithNpcOfType: explicit false for both and hidden native dialogs clear the visit, allowing a later repeatable pass with the same NPC. Restricted/missing interaction state retains history conservatively; no polling is added. Individual quest steps/options run once per conversation, with at most twenty automatic actions. Creation waits outside combat; combat dialogs, world changes, disable and confirmations cancel state without later replay. No protected widget mutation, native replacement, CVar change or automatic confirmation handler is installed.

26 Lua 5.1 files, matching TOCs and 2,868 checks pass, including 158 new assertions for both quest-list models, correct ID/index usage, dialogue timing, independent switches, modifiers, reward/cost/PvP/manual cases, restricted/missing/nonfinite data, late reward metadata, synchronous reentrancy, repeatable and option loops, burst coalescing, close/combat/world/disable cancellation and fourteen-tab footer clearance. Native rendering, ordinary-addon action availability, interaction continuations, taint and saved persistence remain pending in testing.md.

## Whole-addon review — October 9, 0.1.73

The user requested a complete review after accepting the ordinary bag-opening correction ("Perfect"). Reviewed all 25 shipped Lua files, the two TOCs and XML packaging, focusing on opening/layout order, control clearance, reversible native ownership, enable/disable cycles, queued work, combat and data readability. This is a source/substitute audit, not a new running-client verification. Installed WowB.exe was re-read as 1.60.1.70334; running GetBuildInfo()/Interface remains unreported.

Five concrete areas needed changes:

- General Windows still deferred saved Character/Mail/Merchant/Calendar/other supported panel placement. Extend the bank's guarded synchronous path to supported non-bag windows. Native FramePositionDelegate.UpdateUIPanelPositions calculates sibling widths using scales while updatingPanels is true: wait until the completed method post-call before holding saved geometry. Capture first/late native reset anchors and scale, keep later headers coalesced, and preserve active drags, unsaved defaults, forbidden/restricted values and combat deferral. Bags retain their separate completed container pass; no panel registration, manager state or native functions are replaced.
- Native new/temporary chat creation previously deferred discovery/styling. Native FCF creation finishes copying settings and docking before its post-call, so apply there outside combat. Existing OnShow restores style/input alignment immediately; events stay coalesced and combat stays deferred. Preserve native history, input, copy delegation and the two existing account defaults.
- Meter Layout showed each frame before Position assigned the final anchors. Position both Suite-owned windows before showing either. This guarantees settled geometry to show handlers; no live renderer defect is claimed from the substitute alone.
- Square minimap restore records retained the first native geometry, fonts, artwork visibility, shape provider and clamping across re-enable. Release restored snapshots and recapture the current native state on each enable, retaining separate one-time hook registries. Use one cancellable next-frame timer, cancel on settings/combat entry, and keep disabled hooks idle. Validate readable finite snapshot geometry and predicates. Native widget parents/scripts, coordinate band and CVar stay owned by Blizzard.
- Item upgrade hints compared missing required levels and could index/compare restricted item/stat/equipped data. Validate required/player/item levels, weighted stats and equipped links before using them. Incomplete/restricted/nonfinite data produces no hint, preserving the disclosed stat model and existing asynchronous metadata path. Self-tooltip averaging also rejects restricted equipped links.

Version 0.1.72 addresses the user's report that bags briefly open at Blizzard's default location before snapping to their saved position. First combat openings retain the prior Lua-only record and current readable false protection requirement; forbidden/hidden/unsaved/restricted geometry, disabled tools and active drags retain their guarded behavior. Full readable ContainerItemInfo.hyperlink preserves instance attributes. Keep a whole previous bank type when required data is unavailable/secret. Never serialize raw API tables, transient locks/cooldowns or hidden values. BANKFRAME_CLOSED cancels work without scanning already-cleared containers. ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT drives event retries; request each pending ID at most once per bank visit so failure does not create an idle retry loop.

Add personal bankView and per-region accountBankViews to the existing inventory schema without resetting its aggregate snapshots or toggles. Offline rendering never invokes container reads or bank transactions. Set the intrinsic item's current itemLink explicitly, leaving bagID/itemLocation unset; native GetBagID/GetItemLocation methods can exist and return nil. Use GameTooltip:SetHyperlink with the saved instance link, ownership-aware clearing and modifier events for comparisons. Forward only CHATLINK/DRESSUP modified clicks through HandleModifiedItemClick; install no pickup/use/split/receive-drag scripts. Creation/anchors/size/tab changes defer in combat, and disabled events/timers/deferred work cancel. General Windows discovery includes the Suite-owned viewer; no UIPanelWindows mutation or secure-function replacement is needed.

## Source hierarchy

The running client settles availability and behavior. Its matching Blizzard-generated API documentation supplies argument types, return shapes, nil behavior, secret predicates and access requirements; FrameXML then shows how Blizzard uses those APIs. General addon documentation supplies conventions, and third-party addons provide examples that still need testing.

The complete matching Lua, XML and TOC source is cached in `.cache/wow-ui-source/Interface/AddOns` from [Blizzard's UI source mirror at build 70245](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/tree/15666a6e67938a1ab5caf041406464251db111ca). The [70245 commit](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/commit/15666a6e67938a1ab5caf041406464251db111ca) and [earlier baseline comparison](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/compare/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1...a84e2b1b41d3d4137127c07e4da448aa3251d6f1) establish source identity; historical links below remain valid. Shared source files can contain APIs for other game families or secure-only code. A function being documented does not make it callable by an ordinary addon.

The [community Forever API reference](https://atraeau.github.io/WoW-Addons/) is also cached as `.cache/forever-api.json`. Its exported metadata identifies build 70170, rather than this installation's 70245. It indexes 6,598 functions and 1,806 events, but its simplified records omit critical restrictions. Use it to find a name, then check the matching Blizzard source and client behavior. The author's [generation guide](https://github.com/Atraeau/WoW-Addons/blob/main/docs/README.md) explains the in-game documentation export.

## Addon files and runtime

WoW loads Lua and XML in TOC order. A development environment, external program or web service is not part of the addon runtime. Put each shipped addon in its own folder under `Interface/AddOns`; the folder and TOC names must match. Keep the plain and `Camelot` TOCs synchronized. Declared SavedVariables belong in the TOC, and libraries must load before code that depends on them.

Use Lua 5.1-compatible syntax and the WoW runtime's available extensions. Do not assume filesystem, HTTP, shell execution, an ordinary desktop Lua package system or an unrestricted `require` function. An addon can use Blizzard's APIs, bundled files, SavedVariables and permitted addon messaging. External research and packaging tools remain development tools.

The `local addonName, namespace = ...` convention shares a private table across an addon's Lua files without creating a new global for each module. Keep globals limited to the declared saved table, slash command registration and necessary compatibility entry points. Avoid collisions with Blizzard or other addons.

## Startup and saved settings

Initialize this addon's saved data when its own `ADDON_LOADED` event fires. Use `PLAYER_LOGIN` for work requiring a logged-in player, and the appropriate later events for world state, newly loaded Blizzard panels and item information. Hidden or load-on-demand windows can be absent at startup. Checking once during initial Lua execution is insufficient. The matching [addon event documentation](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/AddOnsDocumentation.lua) and [system events](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SystemDocumentation.lua) provide the current event names.

SavedVariables are serialized by the client around logout or UI reload. Keep persistent values simple: booleans, numbers, strings and ordinary tables, with a schema version and defaults that preserve existing preferences. Do not save frame objects, callbacks, cyclic data or secret values. Add explicit migrations when a setting's meaning changes.

A [firsthand beta persistence report](https://us.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/uiaddon-settings-wiped-on-client-restart/2353992) describes settings failing to load on earlier builds. It does not establish that build 70205 has the same defect. Test `/reload`, character relog and full client restart separately. Avoid modifying the user's saved files or introducing external restoration workarounds without a demonstrated need.

## Modern API mapping

| Older pattern | Forever development approach |
| --- | --- |
| Global `GetSpellInfo` | `C_Spell.GetSpellInfo`, which returns a record and can return nothing |
| Global `GetSpellCooldown` tuple | `C_Spell.GetSpellCooldown`, which returns a record with restricted data in some contexts |
| Global bag item tuple APIs | `C_Container.GetContainerItemInfo`, which returns `ContainerItemInfo` |
| Global item information helpers | Prefer the documented `C_Item` function and check each function's specific return shape |
| `OnTooltipSetItem` as the universal entry point | `TooltipDataProcessor.AddTooltipPostCall` with the correct tooltip data type |
| Numeric Interface range used as a modern-client test | Check concrete capabilities and build provenance; Forever's Interface is below Retail's modern range |
| A classic combat-log reader copied unchanged | Check event restrictions, environment and callback requirements before attempting a combat feature |

These mappings come from the matching [spell API](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SpellDocumentation.lua), [container API](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/ContainerDocumentation.lua), [item API](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/ItemDocumentation.lua) and [tooltip processor](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_SharedXMLGame/Tooltip/TooltipDataHandler.lua).

## Secure code and combat lockdown

WoW's secure UI protects gameplay actions and selected frames. Ordinary addon code cannot freely cast spells, choose targets or rewrite protected action behavior during combat. A secure action template permits defined actions through its supported attributes and permitted user interaction; it does not grant general automation privileges.

Cosmetic changes still need care when they reach a protected frame or one of its dependencies. Check `InCombatLockdown()` before changing protected anchors, scale, movement, parenting or configuration. Queue the latest requested change and apply it after `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED`. A frame can also be forbidden; skip it rather than probing it through repeated errors.

Observe Blizzard behavior with supported hooks and keep module callbacks reversible where possible. Replacing secure functions, altering panel-manager globals or modifying restricted frame scripts can taint Blizzard execution, causing an action to be blocked later. `hooksecurefunc` does not make code in its callback secure. Catching an error with `pcall` does not grant access. The matching [secure template implementation](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_FrameXML/SecureTemplates.lua) is a reference for supported behavior, not a template to transplant wholesale.

## Secret values and access predicates

Secret values hide information from ordinary addon logic while allowing specified native presentation paths. Check `issecretvalue` or appropriate access predicates before comparing, calculating, formatting, indexing by or exporting a potentially restricted value. Guard each value that an operation uses; guarding one field does not authorize the rest of its record.

Restrictions depend on the API, unit, spell, map and active combat or encounter state. A value readable in town can be restricted later, and individual spells can override general secrecy rules. Check the documented `SecretArguments`, `SecretWhen...`, `Requires...`, `Environment`, `NeverSecret` and method protection markers. UI objects can acquire secret aspects when they display restricted data. A widget accepting a value does not make the value readable again.

For presentation, use the explicitly supported native path. For example, status bars have documented secret argument support; spell and aura duration objects offer native timing paths subject to the receiving widget's access rules. Do not calculate hidden cooldowns by subtracting timestamps or reinterpret a display object as an unrestricted data source. Consult the matching [FrameScript predicates](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/FrameScriptDocumentation.lua), [secret predicates](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SecretPredicatesDocumentation.lua), [status bar API](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/SimpleStatusBarAPIDocumentation.lua) and [cooldown widget API](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/FrameAPICooldownDocumentation.lua).

The matching combat-log documentation still contains `COMBAT_LOG_EVENT_UNFILTERED` with `HasRestrictions` and `CallbackEvent` markers. Do not describe it as universally absent just because an older export omits it, or assume its presence restores older addon access. `C_CombatLogSecure` is marked `SecureOnly`. See the matching [combat-log declarations](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/CombatLogDocumentation.lua) and [secure combat-log API](https://github.com/Gethe/wow-ui-source/blob/e3ecc27b64d30fdc735a3f6579b866858f9f9df1/Interface/AddOns/Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/CombatLogSecureDocumentation.lua).

## API areas for the requested features

| Feature | Reference area | Main development concern |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Square minimap | `Blizzard_Minimap/Camelot/Skin.lua`, minimap widget documentation, `C_Map` | Blizzard can reapply the mask; restore decorations correctly and preserve controls |
| Minimap stats | Frame rate, network stats, map position, clock APIs | Slow ticker, missing coordinates and readable-value guards |
| Window mover | Frame anchors and scale, panel lifecycle, load-on-demand events | Combat lockdown, panel-manager reflow, default restoration and unusual windows |
| Icon item levels | `C_Item`, `C_Container`, `ItemButtonMixin` and individual item surfaces | Cached data, pooled buttons, missing links and methods copied from mixins |
| Item tooltip details | `TooltipDataProcessor`, `C_TooltipInfo`, `C_Item` | Repeated callbacks, comparison tooltips and clearing old annotations |
| Player details | Unit identity APIs, `NotifyInspect`, `INSPECT_READY`, `C_PaperDollInfo` | Restricted identity, inspect range, throttling and concurrent inspect users |
| Stat upgrade hints | `C_Item.GetItemStats`, inventory slots and usability | Class and role preferences, ring pairs, empty slots and unmodeled effects |
| Configuration GUI | Original frames and controls; native Settings supports a launch category if wanted | Layout, readable text, reusable controls and clear current state |
| Recipe macros | Native `Blizzard_MacroUI`, `C_Item`, `C_Container`, `C_TooltipInfo` | Explicit creation, General ownership, incomplete data, native editor and combat lockdown |
| Damage meters | `C_DamageMeter`, native menu, font-string and status-bar APIs | Secret-aware native display, retained session IDs, combat deferral and independent layout ownership |

The matching minimap source exposes `MinimapCluster.MinimapContainer.PlayerCoords`, anchored with its bottom 18 units below the map. Its frame remains shown when coordinates are disabled; `showPlayerCoords` controls whether its text is populated. Anchor the Suite stats row six units below that frame, leave its anchors and CVar alone, and suppress Suite's fallback coordinates while the native display is enabled. The compact Suite header anchors both lower corners two units above the outer minimap border, matching its full width automatically through size and scale changes; reanchor the native zone/tracking/calendar/clock widgets while retaining their parents and scripts, and save anchors, dimensions, scale and rendering layers for restoration. Keep the native zone button's click behavior and tooltip. Draw a centered 12-point warm-gold area name with a centered 10-point muted subzone label below it; refresh on zone events and reject restricted strings. Native tracking and calendar controls remain at the left/right ends. The native clock moves eight units inside the map at bottom center with a 14-point outlined font; preserve and restore its font as well as its anchors. Header and stats backgrounds use 0.65 opacity and a one-unit border matching the map. With the clock removed, labels have more room. Measure unbounded text width, fit fonts to readable minimums, and wrap longer names while expanding the header height; never abbreviate them. Normal names retain the 36-unit bar. Hide and restore the original one-line zone text. Do not copy ZoidsTools' header implementation or style; it is a local behavior reference authorized by the user.

Version 0.1.48 adds home latency to the existing world display, following the October 8 request for `XX FPS / H: XX ms W: XX ms`. The cached 70245 `ConnectionDocumentation.lua` declares the trailing latency list, and `Blizzard_PerformanceBar/PerformanceBar.lua` explicitly assigns home/world to returns three/four of `GetNetStats`. Read both once per existing one-second stats tick, guard each with the existing readable-number predicate, and omit unavailable/restricted values independently. Keep the latency pair in one stats segment, preserve optional FPS/fallback fields and leave native coordinates and the clock under their existing ownership. Native appearance and running-client behavior still require a smoke test.

Item data is asynchronous. Request uncached data once, listen for the appropriate completion event and refresh only relevant visible surfaces. A pooled button can represent a different item on its next use, so clear old text and recalculate from the current link or item location. Do not identify an item from its icon texture.

The October 6 missing-icon report exposed a native source detail: `Blizzard_ItemButton/Shared/ItemButtonTemplate.lua` implements `GetItemLink` as a read of `self.itemLink`, but `Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Mainline/ContainerFrame.lua:UpdateItems` passes the current hyperlink only to `SetItemButtonQuality`. It does not populate that field. Therefore an inherited `GetItemLink` can return nil (or a stale value) even on a populated bag slot. Resolve readable bag/slot coordinates first, then equipment `GetItemLocation`, and only then a generic getter or current supplied identifier. Native bank slots use `GetBankTabID`/`GetContainerSlotID`; the bank panel is `BankFrame.BankPanel`. `EnumerateValidItems` yields index/button for bags, whereas the bank's object pool yields button/active-marker from `pairs`; do not select a truthy marker as the button.

Version 0.1.24 optionally uses the documented `C_Item.GetCurrentItemLevel` for a readable current location, falling back to link/cached levels only when the result is unavailable. Guard location fields and existence/current-level results before using them; a restricted current level leaves the number blank. Native `UpdateItems`, bank generation/refresh and container show hooks refresh visible slots, retaining global and per-button quality hooks for other surfaces. Post-hook native `Reset` after it has cleared item fields. Draw the quality-colored number at TOPRIGHT with a two-unit inset, outlined font and OVERLAY sublevel 7. `CreateFontString` documents three arguments; set its sublevel separately through `SetDrawLayer`. Restore neutral white if quality is absent/restricted rather than retaining a previous rarity color. Use one cancellable event timer and preserve addon-discovery work when coalescing events; disabling clears timers and deferred creation. No idle updates or Retail upgrade-track API are added.

The user authorized reading `P:\GitHub Repos\ZoidsTools\Modules\ItemOverlays.lua` and its MIT license as a behavior reference for this correction. Forever Suite's resolver, hooks and visuals were implemented locally; ZoidsTools was not changed. The API/UI source reviewed at 70205 is identical in the refreshed 70235 baseline. The supplied screenshot has no running-build result, and native icon rendering, taint and item surfaces need an in-game retest.

The October 6 cooking screenshot shows that generic item-button hooks also reach food results and crafting reagents. Icon levels and item tooltip levels must share `Items:IsEquipment`, using the readable `itemEquipLoc` at position nine of the cached `C_Item.GetItemInfo` tuple. Include armor, jewelry, shirts/tabards and every supported weapon/off-hand/ranged type; exclude empty/unknown types, bags, quivers and ammunition. This is independent of the current character's usability or required level. Clear previous button text before classifying or waiting for item data, and reject restricted item levels/quality before comparison, display or color lookup. A restricted supplied identifier must discard the previous recorded item rather than reuse it. No new API was introduced; the running-client retest remains required.

Modern tooltip post-calls receive both the tooltip and its `tooltipData`. On this build, `ShoppingTooltipTemplate` mixes in `TooltipDataHandlerMixin` without `GameTooltipDataMixin`, whose temporary compatibility methods supply `GetItem` and `GetUnit`. Therefore a global item post-call must not assume those methods exist. Prefer a readable item-instance GUID resolved with `C_Item.GetItemLinkByGUID`, then the data's hyperlink, and finally an available tooltip method or readable item ID. Reset annotation bookkeeping on `OnTooltipCleared`. This distinction is documented by the matching `Blizzard_GameTooltip/Mainline/GameTooltip.xml`, `GameTooltip.lua` and `Blizzard_SharedXMLGame/Tooltip/TooltipUtil.lua` in the cached source; the user's comparison tooltip crash confirmed it in game.

The native bag manager's `UpdateContainerFrameAnchors` reapplies each shown bag's scale and points when its layout changes. Observe these calls and restore saved positions after the native pass. General layout work coalesces and protected mutations defer during combat; version 0.1.44 adds a narrow synchronous restore for known native bags whose current `IsProtected()` is readably false. A bag reset/disable should let that manager recompute the default layout for the currently open bags rather than relying only on startup anchors.

Excluding the bag's entire anonymous title router leaves only a narrow lower strip for movement. Instead, recognize the native `routeToSibling = "PortraitButton"` child and share its title surface. Record a readable cursor hit on left-button down, cancel it on drag, wheel input or hide, and forward the original router mouse-down callback on a matching release. This preserves its menu without replacing native scripts. Keep the portrait and close button excluded. The cached menu manager handles global mouse-down, so opening on release avoids that earlier outside-click dispatch. Cursor checks must use effective scale and reject restricted geometry; combat cancels forwarding.

Native title dragging must preserve header inputs. `ContainerFramePortraitButtonRouterTemplate` is an anonymous clickable child across the bag's `TitleContainer`, forwarding mouse-down to `PortraitButton`; excluding only named controls misses it. Enumerate child frames and subtract visible input-control bounds, including `GetHitRectInsets`, from the drag regions. Convert each child's `GetRect` with its effective scale into the window's units, and guard every geometry value. Refresh after relevant control show/hide, size and anchor changes, coalescing events. If an input control's bounds are restricted, leave the header untouched. Missing coordinates on an unlaid-out child or a passive decoration must not disable the whole header. Calendar needs two base regions following its raised central title and lower side bars; the matching `Blizzard_Calendar/Mainline/Blizzard_Calendar.xml` defines the arrows, filter and close positions.

Blizzard's portrait template uses level 500 for NineSlice and 510 for TitleContainer and close buttons. Input at merely parent-level + 1 can sit underneath these native frames. Parent transparent drag frames to the target window so scale and visibility follow it, exclude those owned frames from child scans, and place input above the visible native header layers. Preserve explicit modal blockers. Ctrl+wheel input needs mouse-wheel reception enabled and a modifier guard; sizing must preserve the saved center, clamp scale and pause during combat. The user authorized inspection of `P:\GitHub Repos\ZoidsTools\Modules\MovableWindows.lua` on October 5; its parented input frames and wheel hooks were used as an implementation reference. Its MIT license is in that repository. No source or GUI was transplanted into this suite.

Do not assume Retail gameplay systems exist because a `C_` namespace or common UI file exists. Verify Forever's spells, ranks, equipment slots, banks, stats, talents and class-specific behavior. Likewise, nil inspect data is not an item level of zero. Inspect requests should pause when the unit changes, the tooltip disappears, another inspect takes over or the necessary context is unavailable.

If a current slot exists but its hyperlink is not loaded, use the readable ID from `C_Container.GetContainerItemInfo` or `C_Item.GetItemID` for that same location so `GetInfo` can request its asynchronous data. Never reuse the previous supplied item for an empty slot, or fall back past a restricted link/ID.

SimpleTextureBase SetTexture/SetAtlas/SetTexCoord and SimpleStatusBar SetStatusBarColor explicitly permit tainted presentation. Use the existing texture's SetTexture instead of SetStatusBarTexture, whose CheckAllowChangeParent can affect hierarchy. Restoring SetAtlas passes useAtlasSize=false so it never changes protected dimensions. Non-atlas external texture replacements keep their pixels and restore the native tint, rather than receiving a stale atlas. Module enable captures current native state after any disabled changes. The controller, native hooks and discovery are constructed outside combat; existing cosmetic painting/restoration can continue in combat. Events coalesce in one cancellable timer outside combat, and the core queue handles combat discovery. Disabled retained hooks return before unit queries; no new OnUpdate is installed.
