## Camping bar — October 6 implementation

The user's screenshots establish the English **Campfire Nearby** proximity name and a Lodestone use tooltip requiring a nearby campfire. A camping reward or countdown is not proof that a camp is still nearby. The module matches the screenshot name and reference ID 1283391, with `C_Spell.GetSpellName` for that ID's localized name. Do not add unrelated camping aura IDs without confirming their meaning in the current client. Read helpful player auras only outside combat through `C_UnitAuras.GetAuraDataByIndex`; its matching documentation declares `RequiresUnitAuraAccess` and `SecretWhenUnitAuraRestricted`. Errors, inaccessible records and secret fields cannot establish proximity. The live aura ID and behavior remain unverified.

The [official beta notes](https://us.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/wow-forever-beta-development-notes-%E2%80%93-updated-october-1/2360696), checked October 6, confirm sitting, sitting in a chair, sleeping, lying down and the kneeling emote qualify for camp buffs. Suite supplies the four pose commands through a hardware-clicked `SecureActionButtonTemplate` using `type1=macro` and `macrotext1`, with a native localized emote alias when available. This follows the cached `SecureTemplates.lua` macro action path, which calls restricted `C_Macro.RunMacroText` from secure execution. Suite never calls that API directly and never performs an emote on selection or from events. It does not create General macros. Chair interaction stays native.

The carried-item scanner uses `C_Container.GetContainerItemInfo`, `C_Item.GetItemInfo` (class is return 12), `C_Item.GetItemSpell`, and supplied `C_TooltipInfo.GetBagItem` lines. It discovers English nearby-fire requirements rather than copying a third-party item catalog, excludes recipes/no-use items and requests asynchronous metadata once. Counts combine by readable item ID. Secure `type1=item` / `item1=item:ID` actions preserve native targeting, capacity, profession requirements and cooldown rules. `C_Item.GetItemCooldown` returns two numbers and a **boolean**, unlike `C_Container.GetContainerItemCooldown`'s numeric enable flag; guard all three fields before using a native Cooldown widget. No hidden timing is calculated.

Protected UI construction, anchors, attributes, resizing, movement and layout are limited to out-of-combat code. A `SecureHandlerStateTemplate` parent with frame references hides both the bar and pose chooser on its native combat state. Both referenced frames use `SecureHandlerBaseTemplate,BackdropTemplate` from creation; register the driver after their references and UI are ready. Combat exit reveals only the parent, retaining the child's hidden state until fresh proximity is read. Insecure events cancel timers and defer protected drag cleanup. The position preview has no secure descendants, takes its own mouse input and closes when settings close or combat begins. Only the two Escape-managed preview/flyout frames have global names; remaining state uses Suite. No Blizzard secure functions or panel-manager globals are changed.

The user's October 6 14:39:36 combat error reached `RestrictedFrames.lua:84` from the controller created in `Camping.lua`. The pose chooser previously used only `BackdropTemplate`; a protected parent does not make its ordinary descendants protected. `RestrictedInfrastructure.lua` distinguishes explicitly protected handles from other handles, and `RestrictedFrames.lua:GetPossiblyForbiddenHandleFrame` rejects an unprotected target in combat even when called from a secure snippet. The old test substitute incorrectly inherited protection downward and passed raw frames into the snippet, masking the defect. It now models protected ancestors and separate restricted handles, reproducing `Invalid frame handle` before the correction. The current cache metadata records commit `a84e2b1b41d3d4137127c07e4da448aa3251d6f1`, build 70235, with the Interface/AddOns sources unchanged from 70205. Running build/Interface and the corrected live combat result remain unreported.

Camping originally used the cached build 70205 revision. Refreshed metadata now records 70235 with identical addon sources, matching the installed executable observation; no fresh running `GetBuildInfo()` capture is available. Read-only EverCore behavior review and offline substitutes establish development intent, not native secure legality, item coverage, combat taint or visual correctness. Record those results in the smoke log before calling camping verified.

## Character frame item level — October 6

Version 0.1.37 uses the matching 70245 `Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Camelot/PaperDollFrame.xml` and `.lua`. `CharacterModelScene` fills the 398-unit left pane and has level 50; its native control row starts ten units below the top and is 32 units high. A passive anonymous child at scene level + 1 spans the pane with 64-unit side insets, begins 48 units below its top and contains one outlined 14-point centered font string. It retains the native scene, controls, level/class font string, anchors and scripts. `PaperDollFrame_ShowSidebar` changes that same scene to a pet on `CharacterStatsPanePetScrollBox`; a guarded post-hook refresh hides the player's label on that sidebar.

The matching `PaperDollFrame_SetItemLevel` consumes the second return of the global `GetAverageItemLevel` as equipped average; the first includes carried gear and the third is PvP. That legacy global is used in native source but has no declaration in this cached generated API documentation, so source usage alone does not prove ordinary-addon availability. Capability-check it, validate only the equipped result before arithmetic/formatting, and display an em dash for absent, zero, nonfinite or restricted values without inferring a number from the other returns. Round to a whole number as requested for the Suite's existing item-level presentation. This module does not change the separate self-tooltip averaging method or issue inspections.

`PaperDollInfoDocumentation.lua` declares `PLAYER_AVG_ITEM_LEVEL_UPDATE` and `PLAYER_EQUIPMENT_CHANGED`; combine them with world entry and addon discovery through one cancellable 0.05-second timer, with no idle update or item scanner. Disabled modules install no hooks/events; retained hooks check the preference. Native OnShow/OnHide scripts remain hooked, with hide cancelling pending timers. Module Apply and refresh use the existing combat queue for creation, anchoring, sizing and presentation; disable cancels event/timer/deferred-enable work. The installed executable was re-read as 1.60.1.70245 during this change. The user's screenshot has no running build/Interface result. Native average availability, model layering/clearance, pet transitions, scaling, combat taint and persistence still need the recorded client smoke test.

## Full button range indicator — October 7

Version 0.1.39 uses the cached 70245 `Blizzard_ActionBar/Shared/ActionButton.lua`: `ActionBarButtonRangeCheckFrameMixin` owns native `EnableActionRangeCheck` subscriptions, dispatches `ACTION_RANGE_CHECK_UPDATE` by slot and invokes `ActionButton_UpdateRangeIndicator(button, checksRange, inRange)`. `Blizzard_ActionBar/Shared/PetActionBar.lua` passes the eighth/ninth returns from `GetPetActionInfo` to the same callback on its existing native update cadence. Suite post-hooks that presentation callback without subscribing/unsubscribing native range checks or adding polling. Native keybind text/color, cooldowns, counts and secure action behavior remain native.

`ActionBarFrameDocumentation.lua` declares `HasAction` and `IsActionInRange` with `RequiresValidActionSlot` and secret arguments allowed only when untainted. `IsActionInRange` returns a nilable boolean: nil means range is undetermined, for example without a target. Validate a readable positive finite integer `button.action`, capability-check the APIs and reject restricted presence/range before testing booleans. Native button `Update`/show and initial discovery seed the current range, covering paging, item/macro replacements and already-out-of-range targets. Pet lookup is capability-checked against the matching native return use; the cached generated pet documentation does not declare this legacy function, so ordinary-addon availability requires a client test. `checksRange` must be readable true and `inRange` readable false before tinting. Hidden/empty buttons and unknown/restricted results clear the Suite tint.

`SimpleRegionAPIDocumentation.lua` declares `SetVertexColor` as `AllowedWhenTainted` with VertexColor/Alpha secret aspects. `GetVertexColor` can return nothing or secret channels. Readability-check every RGB/alpha component before any nil test, comparison or caching; tint only if the current native color can be restored. Observe the icon setter to retain native white/gray/mana-blue recolors while an out-of-range button stays red; a recursion guard excludes Suite's own writes. Restricted native setter arguments stay on the native path and are never cached, inspected through text, or replaced with a guessed color. Runtime color records and range flags never enter SavedVariables.

Discover known main/multibar/override/extra/pet globals without enumerating arbitrary protected descendants. Install global/texture/button post-hooks and show/hide observers outside combat, retaining native scripts and never changing attributes, frame layout or parenting. The one feature event frame is constructed outside combat. Coalesce world/addon discovery through one cancellable 0.05-second timer; entering combat cancels it and leaves one core deferred callback. Existing cosmetic updates and disabling restore colors through the permitted texture setter during combat. First enable/new discovery waits until combat ends. Disabled callbacks return before API queries and feature events/timers are removed. Source matches installed WowB.exe re-read October 7 as 1.60.1.70245; running build/Interface, native visuals/range behavior, taint and persistence remain unverified.

## Vendor tools — October 7

Version 0.1.40 added original vendor controls against the installed 1.60.1.70245 executable and identical cached addon-source baseline; the executable was re-read October 7 during the 0.1.42 popup change. `Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/MerchantFrameDocumentation.lua` declares `C_MerchantFrame.IsSellAllJunkEnabled`, `GetNumJunkItems` and `SellAllJunkItems` in the All environment without a secure-only marker. `Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Mainline/MerchantFrame.lua:1124–1132` confirms the native Sell Junk button presents a generic confirmation with the exact `MerchantFrame_OnSellAllJunkButtonConfirmed` callback, which invokes `SellAllJunkItems`.

The user explicitly requested automatic acceptance when permanent selling is enabled. Version 0.1.42 removes Suite's own per-sale confirmation and directly requests one native bulk sale per active visit when autoSellJunk and Instant are both enabled; the settings retain the no-recovery warning. Install a supported post-hook on `StaticPopup_ShowCustomGenericConfirmation` outside combat only when that option is enabled, without replacing the native function, button, handler or popup registry. Readability-check supplied data/callback before table/type/equality use, then require callback identity with the current native junk confirmation function. No text heuristic, broad generic-popup acceptance or callback replacement is used. Recheck current settings, combat, shown merchant, readable true native Merchant interaction, bulk capability and junk count before invoking the recognized callback. `Blizzard_StaticPopup/StaticPopup.lua:450–460` accepts an exact data table in `StaticPopup_Hide`; hide only GENERIC_CONFIRMATION with that same native data, preserving other warnings. A disabled/Slow/repair-only observer returns without effects, and completed automatic sales do not repeat merely from money/bag events or combat transitions. Late popup helpers are discovered on addon-load events without polling. Native callback access, popup visibility and combat taint remain pending live checks.

The matching native merchant source sets `BUYBACK_ITEMS_PER_PAGE = 12` and uses ordinary `C_Container.UseContainerItem` for bag sales. Slow mode caps attempts at 12 per active visit, with one sale every 0.2 seconds or longer and at most two seconds waiting for each previous slot to change. Snapshot readable ID/count pairs, then revalidate each current slot and native bag exclusions before use; unknown/missing data never selects an item. The current container record supplies quality, isLocked and hasNoValue, so no asynchronous `C_Item.GetItemInfo` price assumption is needed. Nil/unknown/restricted records stay in bags. Scan only carried bag IDs 0 through the readable equipped-bag limit, bounded to 5; no bank inventory is touched. `ContainerDocumentation.lua` declares GetBackpackSellJunkDisabled/GetBagSlotFlag/UseContainerItem, and `BagConstantsDocumentation.lua` declares ExcludeJunkSell. Fail closed if exclusion predicates are missing or restricted. Cursor-held items and the native manual repair cursor block the slow use path. Do not automatically sell newly bought-back stacks after completing the snapshot. Buyback is temporary; further manual/automatic sales can displace older entries, so never promise permanent recovery.

`Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Mainline/MerchantFrame.xml:347–383` uses GetRepairAllCost, CanGuildBankRepair, GetGuildBankWithdrawMoney and GetGuildBankMoney, with -1 allowance meaning unlimited and the effective amount bounded by actual bank funds. Its tooltip explicitly explains that RepairAllItems(true) can charge personal funds for a guild shortfall. Suite's Guild choice requires full permitted guild coverage before that native request and has no intentional personal fallback. Personal requests require readable GetMoney >= cost. These legacy repair globals lack declarations in the cached generated API docs; capability-check them and guard all queried values. No return value proves successful repair, so mark the request once per visit rather than inventing success/cost receipts. Native server funding behavior, balance freshness and ordinary-addon access still require live checks.

One cancellable worker coalesces merchant, bag/lock/flag, player-money and native guild-money events. A MERCHANT_SHOW handler can precede native window visibility; schedule initial work, then validate open-merchant state when it executes. `PlayerInteractionManagerDocumentation.lua` declares `C_PlayerInteractionManager.IsInteractingWithNpcOfType`; require a readable true result for the readable `Enum.PlayerInteractionType.Merchant`, as well as a shown native frame, before actions. A stale visible frame or absent/restricted interaction predicate cannot turn a sale into an ordinary item use. Cancel sale timers/warnings immediately on close, combat, disable or relevant settings change, and validate session identity in all deferred callbacks. Retain the active slow-sale budget through unrelated Suite.Apply calls and combat. Create the event frame after combat, register no feature events while both tools are disabled, and never poll on an idle frame. The source establishes signatures/native usage; it does not verify the running client's sale, Buyback, repair, taint or visual behavior.

## Native cooldown and aura systems

Future combat display features should start by examining the native Cooldown Manager and supported aura containers rather than an unrestricted aura scanner. Blizzard's [October 1 development notes](https://us.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/wow-forever-beta-development-notes-%E2%80%93-updated-october-1/2360696) added Rogue and racial support and addressed duplicate spell-rank entries. Its [known issues](https://us.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/wow-forever-beta-known-issues-october-1/2352687) also describe incomplete tracking. Those details are build-sensitive; native support does not promise coverage of every spell or item.

## Performance and coexistence

Version 0.1.44 responds to the October 7 live report that bags still jump to the default during combat and snap back afterward. The user reports `IsProtected()` false for ContainerFrame1 and ContainerFrame2; running build/Interface were not supplied. Cached 70245 `ContainerFrame.xml` defines numbered and combined containers as movable portrait frames without a secure template, but that does not prove current protection: descendants, anchors or another addon can change it. Check the current predicate on every restore. Matching `SimpleScriptRegionResizingAPIDocumentation.lua` and `SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua` mark SetPoint, ClearAllPoints and SetScale as protected functions; call them in combat only for the whitelisted native bags when protection is readable and exactly false. Do not infer safety from frame name/template alone.

`Windows:HoldBagPositions` runs synchronously after the complete native UpdateContainerFrameAnchors pass, restoring only shown saved native bags. It shares the guarded positioning helper with ordinary out-of-combat ApplyRecord, without calling the header/input layout. Restoring after the complete pass avoids changing the manager's inter-bag anchors while it is still arranging subsequent bags. A reentrancy flag excludes nested post-hook work; a bounded list of combined/numbered names replaces arbitrary frame scans. Newly opened untracked bags can use a Lua-only cache record and acquire normal controls after combat. Forbidden/hidden/unsaved/unknown-protection/restricted-geometry frames skip restoration. Current settings are checked on every callback, disabling waits for general cleanup and then invokes the native bag manager once to rebuild defaults, and protected frames retain the core deferred path. No combat polling, position timer, frame creation, parenting or new SavedVariable is added.

An isolated regression run with the 0.1.43 Windows.lua and current dependencies/tests reproduces the reported jump: the combined bag remains BOTTOMRIGHT during combat instead of CENTER. The corrected source passes the same assertion and 500 complete native-manager passes while creating no timers/frames or header scans. Runtime protection false is confirmed only by the user's supplied predicate results; corrected live retention, taint and CPU/frame-time impact still require testing.

Version 0.1.38 audits combat placement on October 7 against the cached 70245 source and installed WowB.exe, re-read as 1.60.1.70245. `ContainerFrame.lua:UpdateContainerFrameAnchors` can overwrite native bag scale/points; Suite cannot promise an immovable protected native panel if Blizzard reflows it during lockdown. Preserve the saved layout without replacing secure functions, changing `UIPanelWindows`, or polling/reasserting combat anchors. Restore native reflows after combat. Suite-owned meter frames already retain their current geometry without combat positioning; their data refresh timer is separate from layout. Camping retains its secure hide-in-combat behavior, and native Edit Mode still owns minimap positioning.

The native window mover now coalesces record dirtiness and discovery through one cancellable next-frame timer. Retained hooks only set flags; disabled hooks return immediately. `PLAYER_REGEN_DISABLED` cancels that timer and title clicks; combat bursts retain one `windowWork` callback in the core queue, without header scans, handle creation or position timers. The core drains this callback after combat; the mover does not independently rescan on `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED`. First-show discovery, hidden input reconciliation and screen/scale/world changes use the same worker. Out-of-combat completed drags save; hide, disable and combat interruptions discard the unfinished placement and restore the previous committed layout/default afterward.

Matching `SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua` marks `StopMovingOrSizing` as protected. During combat call it only when the existing target's `IsProtected()` result is readable and exactly false; the matching `SimpleScriptRegionAPIDocumentation.lua` marks that predicate's returns secret for ObjectSecurity. Protected/unknown targets defer all movement cleanup. Never save their later cursor location after combat. Forbidden targets discard gesture bookkeeping without mutation or retries. The minimap launcher now observes combat entry to remove its gesture-only updater immediately. Geometry destined for window SavedVariables rejects restricted/nonfinite values and unavailable/zero screen dimensions. Offline burst and mutation guards cover these boundaries; running-client position retention, taint and frame-time impact remain unverified.

Register feature events only when needed, coalesce bursts and cancel timers when a feature turns off. Cache stable information with a clear invalidation point. Avoid scanning every frame, item or aura on every update. An `OnUpdate` callback is appropriate while a user actively resizes a window, not as a permanent polling substitute for events.

Preserve original state before changing it and restore only what this addon owns. Keep references to addon-created decorations separately from Blizzard's objects. Where hooks cannot be removed, disabled callbacks must stay cheap. Test features in isolation, then with the user's installed addons, because two tools controlling the same mask, anchor or tooltip line can conflict without either having a syntax error.

Use third-party libraries only when they remove meaningful complexity. Their presence in another addon does not grant a license to that addon's implementation. Enhance QoL's [upstream license](https://github.com/Numynum/EnhanceQoL/blob/main/LICENSE) is all rights reserved; this suite uses original code and visuals. Blizzard's [addon policy](https://eu.forums.blizzard.com/en/wow/t/wow-user-interface-add-on-development-policy/1642) sets distribution and behavior requirements for addons.

## Validation and maintenance

### Damage meter research — October 6

The installed WowB.exe was re-read as 1.60.1.70235. The source refresh pins commit `a84e2b1b41d3d4137127c07e4da448aa3251d6f1`; its addon sources are identical to the original 70205 reference used for the exploration. Running `GetBuildInfo()` and its Interface remain uncollected. The user approved implementation and requested the Suite color scheme as the default; version 0.1.29 implements it, with native-client verification pending.

`Blizzard_DamageMeter/Blizzard_DamageMeter.toc` explicitly permits `standard, camelot` and depends on Blizzard_EditMode. `DamageMeter.lua` supports up to three native session windows. `SetupSessionWindow` anchors the primary window to the DamageMeter Edit Mode system at both corners; `CanMoveOrResizeSessionWindow` permits only secondary windows to move independently. The native owner fans most appearance settings out to every session window. Do not reparent or detach the primary to implement grouped movement, and do not let the general window mover also own any future Suite meter frames.

`DamageMeterSessionWindow.lua:InitializeDamageMeterTypeDropdown` provides Damage, Healing and Actions category submenus. Its session dropdown obtains `C_DamageMeter.GetAvailableCombatSessions()` when opened and displays the returned fights plus Current and Overall; the user's requested Current expansion changes that hierarchy, not the underlying session model. Use native IDs, not a row index or boss name, for historical selection. Native saved window data deliberately omits `sessionID` because it describes recent encounters. Preserve Suite preferences/layouts only; do not serialize combat values or assume fight IDs survive reload/relog.

`Blizzard_APIDocumentationGenerated/DamageMeterDocumentation.lua` declares `C_DamageMeter` in Environment All. Queries by session type or ID, including source drill-down queries, are `SecretWhenInCombat = true` with `SecretArguments = AllowedWhenUntainted`. GetAvailableCombatSessions supplies ID/name and a nilable duration; it does not promise an unlimited archive. Source GUID/name are conditional secrets; classFilename, specIconID, isLocalPlayer and deathRecapID are explicitly NeverSecret. Numeric totals/rates must be treated as potentially secret. Check the outer record/table and each used field, including IDs, names, durations and API availability results, before branching, formatting, comparing, indexing or saving. Source order comes from the client; do not sort or derive percentages/DPS/HPS from hidden values.

The cached enum defines eleven metric types including Absorbs, while the native category picker exposes ten and omits Absorbs. Confirm the extra metric on the running build before exposing it. Refresh from DAMAGE_METER_COMBAT_SESSION_UPDATED, DAMAGE_METER_CURRENT_SESSION_UPDATED and DAMAGE_METER_RESET, coalescing relevant events and avoiding duplicate refreshes for overall/current notifications. Turn off feature events and cancel timers when disabled. Do not introduce a combat-log parser or idle scanner.

For a Suite-owned presentation, the matching SimpleFontString API marks SetText and SetFormattedText as secret-accepting native widget operations, and SimpleStatusBar marks SetMinMaxValues / SetValue as AllowedWhenTainted with the BarValue secret aspect. Version 0.1.29 sends restricted names and numbers directly to those operations. Compact abbreviation is performed only on readable numbers; restricted numbers use native whole-number formatting. Rates come from `amountPerSecond`, bars use native totals/maxima, and restricted duration is left blank. Guard readability for addon logic and keep layout/input frames independent of data-bearing widgets. Never recover hidden values by reading back native text or bars. Matching documentation supports this implementation; actual in-combat display still requires a client smoke test.

Details was reviewed read-only through the GitHub connector on October 6: `classes/class_instance.lua` (reported modified October 5) and `frames/window_main.lua` (reported modified October 2), plus LICENSE. The moving master ref was used; it is not a pinned repository commit. Exact returned files are retained in `.cache/details-reference`, with SHA256 provenance in `docs/sources.json`; never package them. Its LICENSE states all rights reserved. Read-only behavioral findings: edge proximity guides grouping; left/right grouping matches height; stacked frames align both horizontal ends; grouped resizing propagates dimensions, and ungrouping is a distinct action. Current BaseFrameSnap uses a single root and ordered traversal to avoid circular anchors. Forever Suite's proposed two-member docking geometry and controls must be original, without copying these routines, constants, toolbar or assets. Details' source is a behavior reference, not an API-compatibility authority for Forever.

`DamageMeters.lua` now owns two anonymous, independently configurable frames with slate panels and teal bars by default. Per-window class colors are optional. Forty-eight rows are prebuilt outside combat and a scroll offset displays the full native source list; no data-dependent frame creation or sorting is used. Each header owns metric, session and settings buttons, with empty header space handling drag and Ctrl-wheel. Runtime historical IDs never enter SavedVariables. Available-session failure preserves the previous index without falsely declaring a selected fight evicted.

Docking stores one anchor and side, then computes absolute UIParent rectangles for both frames without cross-anchors. Side joins share height and stacked joins share width; corner resizing, Ctrl-wheel and explicit size edits propagate only the shared dimension. Dragging a joined member moves the pair; Alt-drag or Detach separates it. Candidate preview changes the moving frame's border, with size matching committed on release. Layout changes defer during combat, while native data widgets keep receiving updates. Events coalesce through a cancellable 0.08-second timer; OnUpdate exists only during movement or corner-sizing gestures. Disable unregisters events, cancels work and hides Suite frames. The general mover excludes `foreverMeter` frames.

The module starts off. `/wfs meters` opens its settings. It never changes native meter frames, Edit Mode ownership, recording settings or `damageMeterEnabled`; combat data and retention depend on the client's native meter service. Source/spell breakdowns are added in 0.1.35 below; Absorbs remains outside the exposed native picker. The implementation, acceptance checks and remaining native rendering/taint/persistence tests are recorded in `docs/feature-plan.md` and `docs/testing.md`.

Version 0.1.30 supplies a runtime-only **Test both windows** toggle. Eight original static player records supply sample totals/rates/counts through the same renderer. Test mode bypasses native combat/history queries and temporarily overrides visibility, including when the module is off. It retains real history IDs and saves/restores scroll offsets on runtime window records. Header/footer identify test data. Existing movement, shared-size docking and styling use the same layout owner. Stop restores normal visibility; combat stops preview immediately, restoring native display for enabled windows while their native update events remain registered. Starting a test in combat is rejected; frame/layout work still defers. Preview-only handlers are removed on stop, and no animation/ticker or saved preview flag is introduced. The 70245 mirror refresh changes only version.txt from the reviewed 70235 source, with no new API calls required for sample presentation. Native visuals and combat transitions need the added smoke test.

Version 0.1.31 implements the user's explicit exception to the no-grip native-window rule for Suite-owned meters. Bordered selector buttons have independent arrows and hover feedback; passive six-dot artwork marks a reserved center header gap without intercepting input. Raised lower-left/right handles use existing cursor, frame and texture APIs. `StartResize` snapshots readable rectangles and one dock orientation. `ResizeUpdate` computes free dimensions, holds the opposite top corner, limits the pair to the viewport and updates the partner from the acted-on member without circular anchors. `Layout(rects)` renders temporary dimensions without saving positions during the gesture. Only visible-row-count changes need a data refresh while resizing. `EndResize` commits width/height, shared partner size and normalized positions; `CancelResize` removes the updater and restores the last committed layout, deferring during combat. Pool size 48 covers height 1000 at minimum row height, with no gesture-time construction. User feedback confirms the old selectors/drag area were usable but unclear; it supplies no build or native combat-data result. New hit targets and corner rendering remain subject to live testing.

Version 0.1.32 replaces the six wide settings inputs with inline numeric entries and original teal native sliders. The matching `SimpleSliderAPIDocumentation.lua` documents SetOrientation, SetMinMaxValues, SetValueStep, SetObeyStepOnDrag, SetThumbTexture, GetThumbTexture and SetValue; `Blizzard_SharedXML/Shared/Slider/MinimalSlider.lua` confirms the OnValueChanged callback and programmatic SetValue model. Sliders only receive readable settings values, never combat data. A synchronization flag prevents setter callbacks from writing preferences recursively. GUI-owned pending records capture window/key/value, coalesce live changes through one cancellable 0.05-second timer and flush on release/actions/hide. Typed edits capture the window on focus, clamp to supported ranges and retain displayed fractions to two decimals; percentage values convert to/from stored opacity. Module SetOption remains the layout/combat boundary. No idle updater, new SavedVariable or external asset is added. The annotated screenshot was used for placement only; native control rendering remains pending.

Version 0.1.33 uses the user's two pixelated meter screenshots as visual references only. Original native textures provide a thin 24-unit header, translucent small buttons, muted borders, contiguous bars and unboxed corner marks. Removing the footer changes visible-row capacity to `floor((height - 28) / rowHeight)`; the prebuilt pool remains sufficient at maximum height/minimum row size. Rank labels display the existing source-list index plus the scroll offset without sorting, inspecting hidden amounts or joining restricted names. Readable duration alone is formatted in the metric button and tooltip; narrow layouts hide the time widget rather than compressing controls further. Existing dimensions, colors, fonts and opacity preferences retain their meanings.

The new boolean `meterLocked` is part of the existing Suite saved table. `SetLocked` cancels gestures before hiding their existing unprotected corner/dot widgets; cancellation restores committed rectangles through the existing combat deferral. Both windows share the lock because a docked pair moves together. Guard drag, snap, both resize gestures and background reset before any geometry work. Lock controls remain available during combat and use only presentation setters on prebuilt addon-owned input widgets; they neither construct widgets nor move/scale/anchor frames. Metric/session selection and body scrolling stay functional, while deliberate settings changes retain their normal combat boundary. Padlock artwork is made from original solid textures, with no external asset, new polling or third-party code. General native window tools retain their Ctrl-wheel-only behavior and no lock mode. Substitute checks exercise transitions and combat construction/anchor guards; actual client rendering, input, taint and persistence remain unverified.

For the requested quick reset in 0.1.34, matching `DamageMeterDocumentation.lua` declares `ResetAllCombatSessions()` with no arguments, combat restriction or secure-only marker, and describes clearing all player combat sessions. `DamageMeterSessionWindow.lua:506–507` directly calls it from the native Reset all sessions menu, without a confirmation. Suite's explicit header action uses that same capability-checked shared service, with tooltip wording explaining that both windows and native meters are affected. It reconciles both historical IDs/offsets, cancels any refresh scheduled by a synchronous native reset event and refreshes existing widgets without layout changes. Test mode exits that path before native queries/reset and rewinds sample rows only. Actual ordinary-addon reset behavior, including combat, remains subject to client testing.

The session button displays only C/O in 24 units, preserving full readable session/fight identity in tooltip/menu data. A prebuilt passive TEST label replaces grab dots during sample display and survives locking; it does not intercept header input or add polling. Extra header room goes to the metric title and empty drag gap. All new icon parts are original native solid textures; no assets or library are added. Reset capability is independently checked so absence does not disable ordinary combat display.

Version 0.1.35 implements bar-click source breakdowns in `DamageMeterDetails.lua`, loaded after the meter controller. Matching `DamageMeterDocumentation.lua` declares `GetCombatSessionSourceFromType(sessionType, metric, guid, creatureID)` and `GetCombatSessionSourceFromID(sessionID, metric, guid, creatureID)`. Both are SecretWhenInCombat and permit secret arguments only when untainted. Require readable identifiers before calling them from addon code; never recover GUIDs through names, current ranks or other unit lookups. An unavailable source identifier leaves a native name/total summary and explicit restricted/missing-identity feedback. Readable identifiers captured before combat may still query the normal supported endpoint, subject to the client's returned record restrictions.

The matching native `DamageMeterSourceWindow.lua` consumes `combatSpells`, maxAmount and totalAmount. `DamageMeterEntry.lua:626–635` confirms `combatSpellDetails` is a single unit-details record per spell entry, not a list; display its unitName without aggregating or inventing target statistics. Native amounts/rates/maxima and text use the existing secret-accepting widgets. `C_Spell.GetSpellName` is explicitly AllowedWhenTainted for secret spell arguments, so its result can feed native text without inspecting/recording an opaque ID. Readable missing IDs alone become once-per-selection RequestLoadSpellData keys; SPELL_DATA_LOAD_RESULT uses the existing coalesced refresh while a popup is open. No sorting, percentage/DPS calculation, hidden-value widget readback or combat serialization is introduced.

The original popup and eight rows are prebuilt outside combat and never reanchored/sized on click or refresh. Runtime selection holds only validated identifiers/context and is discarded on close, owner content/hide, resets, disable and test transitions. Test breakdowns use static invented abilities/units. The only new named frame, `WoWForeverSuiteCombatDetails`, is created for standard UISpecialFrames Escape compatibility; its controller/state remain in the private namespace. This is a narrowly scoped compatibility registration like the settings frame, without altering UIPanelWindows or native HUD ownership. Source querying, secret presentation, click/Escape behavior and native combat legality still require the added smoke test; offline substitutes are not proof of those client properties.

Version 0.1.36 pairs main-bar totals with native `DamageMeterCombatSource.amountPerSecond` in parentheses for damage, healing and damage-taken metrics. Rate-only/count metrics retain their existing display. Matching SimpleFontString `SetFormattedText` is explicitly AllowedWhenTainted with the Text secret aspect, including multiple native formatting arguments. Readability is checked independently for total and rate; only readable finite numbers are abbreviated in Lua, while restricted components pass untouched as native numeric arguments. Missing/invalid readable rates are omitted and zero rates retained. Rates are never derived from totals or duration. Out-of-combat layout reserves the value column using only plain width/font settings, without reading secret-bearing text dimensions. Native mixed-secret rendering, visual fit and taint remain unverified.

### Native meter styling and menu isolation — October 7

Version 0.1.41 follows the user's explicit native appearance request. Matching `Blizzard_DamageMeter/DamageMeterEntry.xml` uses NumberFontNormal, `UI-HUD-CoolDownManager-Bar`, `ui-damagemeters-bar-shadowbg` and `ui-damagemeters-bar-shadowedge`. `Blizzard_Fonts_Shared/Shared/FontStyles.xml` inherits NumberFont_Outline_Med, whose Roman member in Mainline/Fonts.xml is Arial Narrow with a normal outline. Resolve the installed NumberFontNormal family for locale support and apply the saved font size, with a guarded Arial fallback. Use the already-installed artwork through native texture operations; no Blizzard implementation or new asset file is packaged. Setup reads the status-bar texture only before combat. Runtime amounts remain exclusively on native SetValue/SetFormattedText paths without measurement or readback.

`DamageMeterEntry.lua:GetIconAtlasElement` uses GetClassAtlas for a source's class. SharedConstants.lua defines the recognized class icon coordinates and atlas formatter; require readable class/table results before any indexing/formatting, clear unknown/restricted identities on recycled rows and scale the icon during normal out-of-combat layout. The header uses the matching MenuTemplates.lua shadowless arrow atlas on a plain twenty-unit Button, with an independent passive gold title and header duration. It does not instantiate a native DropdownButton or change native meter frames.

The user's October 7 17:09:13 stack reaches `Blizzard_Menu/Menu.lua:999` inside MeasureExtents: GetRect returned secret region coordinates, then math.min attempted conversion in execution tainted by Suite. The stack's DropdownButton path is native and does not identify the original taint source. Treat shared-pool involvement as a working diagnosis, not proof of the original cause. `DamageMeterMenus.lua` removes every Suite meter call to MenuUtil/shared menu managers and uses private descriptions plus two prebuilt unprotected popup panels. Labels/session IDs are readability-filtered, dimensions use plain entry counts, and restricted bar/text rectangles are never queried. Frame creation and dismissal propagation setup occur outside combat; only those independent unprotected menu panels may update their plain geometry during combat. No native menu/security function is replaced or hidden data recovered. A named menu frame is registered only for standard Escape compatibility. Opening native dropdowns after Suite choices, in Edit Mode and around combat/reload, remains a required live regression test.

### Meter artwork placement correction — October 7

Version 0.1.42 follows the user's second appearance screenshot. The native arrow already carries a bordered button; adding the generic Suite button backdrop around it creates the uneven nested boxes. Use only its native artwork on a 22-unit hit target, with 25-unit artwork and the center y=-2 offset from `Blizzard_Menu/Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml`. Hover and press use the corresponding shadowless native states; hiding resets transient button state. Native menu infrastructure remains separate.

The matching `DamageMeterEntry.lua:SetupDefaultStyle` insets the fill by one unit vertically and four units at the right. Its background setup and XML edge anchors extend both shadow textures two units beyond the fill. Applying the shadows with SetAllPoints compresses their borders into the fill. Use those native placement offsets, full-row-size icons and native row clipping instead. `SimpleFrameAPIDocumentation.lua` documents SetClipsChildren; configure it only on the Suite-owned rows at construction outside combat. Saved bar height still controls the complete row, without changing preferences, row count or combat-value paths. Native rendering at UI scales remains pending.

### Readable meter text and compact source details — October 7

Version 0.1.43 addresses the user's small/harsh font and strange breakdown design. Native NumberFontNormal's Roman definition uses Arial Narrow at 14 with a normal outline; the old Suite code selected the family but forced size 11. Raise the default to 14 and explicitly use OUTLINE so a shared object's MONOCHROME/THICKOUTLINE flags cannot make Suite rows harsh. Resolve readable nonempty native family paths for locale support. Per-window fontRevision=1 upgrades a legacy 11 once, preserves different custom sizes and permits any later 9–16 choice. The native source popup and main rows now share the family; value-column sizing scales from the new default using plain settings only.

Review `DamageMeterSessionWindow.lua:ShowSourceWindow`, `DamageMeterSourceWindow.lua` and its XML: native opening reanchors the source window, OnShow refreshes a pooled scroll provider, and SetSession belongs to its native session context. `DamageMeterEntry.lua:GetEntryValueText/GetPercentageValue` branches on amounts and divides totals in native execution. Instantiating/calling that implementation from Suite does not make Suite untainted or grant access to hidden values. The user allows a closer native visual presentation, so retain independently owned prebuilt widgets and guarded query/refresh behavior instead of modifying native windows/security functions.

The smaller 420-by-246 popup uses installed common-dropdown-bg, UIPanelCloseButton, meter fill/shadow textures and full-height spell icons. Source/session summary occupies two compact header lines; eight single-line 22-unit rows replace the old 32-unit report columns. Pair amount and supplied rate in one right-aligned field, reversing primary/parenthetical values for DPS/HPS and omitting rates for action counts. Calculate a clamped rounded share only when both finite amount and source total are readable and the total is positive. Hidden totals omit the calculation, including mixed readable/restricted pairs. Name/recipient combinations use secret-accepting native SetFormattedText without concatenating or inspecting hidden strings. C_Spell.GetSpellTexture and SimpleTextureBase:SetTexture explicitly allow secret arguments when tainted; pass opaque icon IDs directly to the native widget. Readable missing data uses a question mark and the existing bounded async name requests. Test icons are static installed assets. Spell hover tooltips require a readable positive ID; recycled/hidden rows clear icon, runtime spell and tooltip state.

Adjacent placement reads only the stored plain meter rectangles and readable UIParent dimensions, chooses the available side and clamps to the viewport outside combat. Combat clicks/refresh preserve the previously configured anchor and create/resize no frames. Existing owner/reset/disable/Escape lifecycle and coalesced events remain. No native popup/frame ownership, protected HUD geometry, saved combat data, idle updater or new shipped asset is introduced. The user's screenshot shows working real source totals/rows but does not supply running build/Interface or establish combat access/taint safety; native appearance and behavior need the added smoke test.

### Meter name width and exact totals/rates — October 7

Version 0.1.46 follows the screenshot comparing Suite's clipped Drock Bracketfix / 16.6k (13) with native Drock Bracketfix / 16,568 (13.5). This is primarily a column-width problem: Suite reserves 140 units even for a shorter value. Matching `DamageMeterEntry.xml` leaves value width implicit, and `DamageMeterEntry.lua:SetupDefaultStyle` anchors its name relative to the value's left edge. Use natural value widths and a four-unit name/value anchor gap on both main/popup rows. Anchors are set once on new empty widgets outside combat, and layout no longer applies fixed widths to these font strings. Native text updates perform their own layout; Suite does not read widths, rectangles or secret-derived extents. Input/parent frames retain plain independently configured dimensions, and private menu sizing remains independent.

Matching `LocalizationDocumentation.lua` marks BreakUpLargeNumbers as AllowedWhenTainted and returning a string. Use it for exact grouped totals, passing its potentially opaque result directly to SetFormattedText. Fractional rates retain one decimal via readable formatting or native %.1f for secret numbers; readable whole rates omit .0. Count/total formats stay integral, and rate-first DPS/HPS popup rows explicitly distinguish primary rate from parenthetical total. Compact remains an optional existing preference. A per-window numberRevision=1 migrates all prior window number choices to the requested Full once; subsequent Compact edits persist. Missing native grouping falls back only for readable values, with normal locale grouping separators; opaque totals retain permitted native whole-number formatting. No hidden value reaches rounding, arithmetic, string processing or serialization. The current native grouping/implicit font geometry paths need live combat/taint verification despite documented permitted operations.

### Meter detail header and outside clicks — October 7

Version 0.1.47 removes the opaque details header that extended beyond the common-dropdown-bg atlas's inset border. The gold player name and native X are directly parented to the popup, with plain inset anchors configured outside combat. Context and spell rows retain their compact layout.

Matching DamageMeterSourceWindow.lua registers GLOBAL_MOUSE_DOWN only while shown, then dismisses non-sticky details when DoesAncestryIncludeAny(self, GetMouseFoci()) is false. InputDocumentation.lua returns a ScriptRegion list; SystemDocumentation.lua declares the synchronous mouse-down event. Use these native capabilities on the independent Suite popup, guarding the list, its entries and the boolean ancestry result before inspecting or branching. An unavailable/restricted focus leaves X/Escape usable. No mouse-coordinate or data-bearing rectangle reads, full-screen input overlay, swallowed outside click, idle update or native frame changes are introduced. OnHide unregisters the listener and runs the existing selection/icon/text cleanup. Native appearance, click propagation and combat/taint still require client testing.

### Shared addon identity artwork — October 6

The cached build 70205 `Blizzard_AddOnList/AddonList.lua` reads the `IconTexture` TOC metadata and renders it at 20 x 20. Without `IconTexture` or `IconAtlas`, it falls back to the question-mark icon shown in the user's screenshot. Both Suite TOCs now declare `Interface\AddOns\WoWForeverSuite\Media\ForeverSuiteIcon.tga`; the minimap launcher uses the same texture. No addon-list hook or native frame modification is needed.

The original gold-and-teal infinity emblem was generated with built-in ImageGen. Keep its PNG master and prompt in `artwork/`, outside the addon package. `scripts/build-icon.py` uses Pillow to downsample to an opaque, uncompressed 128 x 128 32-bit TGA and a review PNG. The launcher draws the texture one unit inside its existing 26-unit button, retaining its teal border and existing edge geometry, interaction and combat deferral. Offline review at 20 and 24 pixels does not verify the client's texture loading or rendering.

The GUI-only wordmark is a separate `Media/ForeverSuiteLogo.tga`. Version 0.1.28 keeps three stacked rows, WARCRAFT / FOREVER / SUITE, with the gold-and-teal infinity centered on and woven primarily through FOREVER. Its loops pass through counters and over/under selected stems, while the other two words stay clear. The earlier user's actual-logo screenshot remains cached only at `.cache/artwork-references/WoW-Forever-user-reference.png`; no font file or reference image is bundled. `scripts/build-icon.py gui` converts `artwork/ForeverSuiteLogo-v4.png` to an opaque uncompressed 256 x 256 32-bit TGA. Previous PNG masters and prompt records remain in artwork. GUI.lua continues to display it through a passive 56 x 56 header texture, 24 units from the left and 10 from the top, with the title/subtitle at x=94/95. It fits within the existing 76-unit header and leaves its drag behavior and raised close button intact. Keep the shared small icon without text, as the user explicitly requested. PNG masters and prompts remain outside the addon package.

Separate syntax, logic and runtime checks. Lua parsing catches invalid source; substitute API tests verify selected behavior; the actual client verifies permitted calls, graphics, taint, events and persistence. Capture the build with every runtime result. Do not call a feature verified based solely on an API listing or an offline test.

When the client changes, compare its build and Interface with the recorded reference, fetch the matching source, recheck changed signatures and predicates, update the TOCs if needed and repeat the affected in-game tests. Repackage only the addon folder. Keep API snapshots, test tools, cached third-party source and user saved data out of the distributable ZIP.

The matching EditModeSystemTemplate declares clampedToScreen=true, and its UpdateClampOffsets adjusts selection bounds. To allow the styled minimap closer to screen edges, preserve the IsClampedToScreen flags of MinimapCluster, Minimap and MinimapBackdrop, then set those flags false outside combat while square styling is enabled. Observe SetClampedToScreen for native reapplication with a recursion guard. Restore original flags when disabled. Leave native clamp insets, Edit Mode ownership, snapping and saved layouts intact; no custom HUD movement or saved anchor system is introduced. ZoidsTools locally confirms the unclamping behavior as a reference.

The original minimap launcher uses a saved angle rather than moving or reparenting the map. Circular placement uses cosine/sine and separate horizontal/vertical radii; square placement divides the ray by its largest component to reach rectangle edges/corners. Convert cursor pixels using Minimap effective scale and center, normalize by those radii, and save the resulting angle. Reject unavailable/restricted/nonfinite geometry. An OnUpdate exists only while dragging; release, hiding or combat ends it. Suppress the click generated after a drag. Defer frame construction/anchors/visibility changes in combat. Hover help uses GameTooltip only while the launcher owns it.

The clarified October 6 launcher placement is outside the map. Square center radii use `map dimension / 2 + button size / 2 + 1`. The square minimap border is already anchored one UI unit outside Minimap in Minimap.lua, so the button's inner sides now meet that border. Round placement starts with map half-dimensions plus one, then extends the normalized ray by `1 + button half-size * (abs(cos) / radiusX + abs(sin) / radiusY)`. This places the square button beyond the circle/ellipse tangent without corner overlap. Normalize dragging by the same square center radii or round base radii, preserving the saved angle. Preserve inherited map scale and the existing combat/drag lifecycle.

For Retail-style item levels, use TooltipDataProcessor.AddTooltipPreCall with readable item tooltip data. The matching handler calls pre-hooks before ProcessLines; TooltipDataLineType.ItemName=22 and ItemLevel=31 identify the insertion point and any native level line. Build a single leftText line with NORMAL_FONT_COLOR, insert after the first item name, and avoid duplicate footer additions in the post-call. Leave restricted line tables/types untouched. Prefer explicit tooltip item GUID/link/id over a tooltip GetItem shim that might still describe an earlier item during a pre-call.

Player equipment levels use AddDoubleLine with a gold Item Level label and white right-column value, as requested October 6. The cached Camelot PaperDollFrame uses this native method with independent left/right colors. Guard the level before rounding it to a whole number; keep the existing self-equipment average and inspect/cache sources. Store the TextRight font string for later updates so repeated callbacks update the value without replacing the label or appending a second row. OnTooltipCleared invalidates that reference. The user confirmed the layout on previously seen players October 6; the running build was not supplied.

The native GameTooltip:GetUnit shim delegates to TooltipUtil.GetDisplayedUnit, which resolves the data GUID solely with UnitTokenFromGUID. For the user's October 6 no-click hover issue, resolve the supplied readable GUID against UnitGUID("mouseover") first, then a validated fallback token, player or the native lookup. Never infer identity from tooltip name text or select a target. The cached Unit documentation marks UnitTokenFromGUID as potentially secret when identity is restricted; guard both token and GUID before use. CanInspect's result also needs a readability check.

For macros, the cached build 70205 `Blizzard_MacroIconSelector.lua` creates with `CreateMacro(name, icon, body, isCharacterMacro)` and edits with `EditMacro(index, name, icon)`. `Blizzard_MacroUI.lua` reads `GetMacroInfo` and `GetNumMacros`, edits text with `EditMacro(index, nil, nil, body)`, and bases character indices above `Constants.MacroConsts.MAX_ACCOUNT_MACROS`. `MacroConstantsDocumentation.lua` declares 120 General and 30 character slots; `Blizzard_MacroUI.xml` limits text to 255 letters. Suite conservatively validates text by bytes and keeps generated names below 16 bytes. Read back the returned index/name/body before recording ownership; never infer ownership from an FS prefix alone. `UPDATE_MACROS` is synchronous in `UIMacrosDocumentation.lua`, so ignore the event during a Suite write and coalesce later work. `C_Macro.RunMacroText` is restricted and is not used. The generator never casts or uses items; users execute saved macros normally from action bars.

The October 6 macro implementation originally used the 70205 source baseline, now confirmed identical in the refreshed installed-70235 reference. No new running-client result was obtained. Item selection uses the current `C_Item.GetItemInfo` tuple (class/subclass are positions 12/13), `GetItemInfoInstant` (class/subclass are 6/7), optional `GetItemSpell`, modern container records, and readable tooltip lines. Request missing item data once and respond to relevant completion events. Mark unreadable/missing APIs or tooltip data as incomplete rather than clearing owned actions. Scan carried bags only, outside combat. Weapon subclass IDs determine ordinary stone families; native item use checks still decide compatibility. Food/potion tooltip classification is English-only; disclose that limitation and allow explicit carried-item choices. Do not read player health/power to rank percentage restoration or inspect cooldowns to choose combat actions.

Version 0.1.45 narrows incomplete metadata to the recipes that depend on it. The unchanged build 70245 `GetItemInfoInstant` contract permits absent returns and places readable class/subclass at 6/7; use this static classification for uncached items outside combat. Do not retry an alternate classification after encountering secret class/subclass values. Unidentified bag contents or inaccessible classification block all writes; readable unknown classes pause recovery selection, while the fixed ordinary-stone and Healthstone ID catalogs determine those dependencies. A selected item's pending ID blocks its own recipe. Combat potion/Healthstone loading only blocks recipes with that addition enabled. This preserves old actions without allowing unrelated uncached reagents to freeze every macro.

Ownership comparison in 0.1.45 normalizes CRLF to LF and ignores terminal empty lines only. Keep every command, condition, internal line and other whitespace intact. Validate actual name and equivalent body after native writes, recording confirmed native text. Meaningful external edits retain their pause rule. `/wfs macrostatus` reads current generation/ownership conditions and reports event/refresh counters; it never creates or edits a native macro. The reported all-recipe update failure was reproduced in API substitutes with an uncached reagent, but its exact live cause and correction remain pending client confirmation.

The user's October 6 runtime error confirmed that EditBox:SetFont requires its third flags argument. The cached `SimpleEditBoxAPIDocumentation.lua` marks flags Nilable=false, while `SimpleFontStringAPIDocumentation.lua` marks FontString flags Nilable=true. Do not assume these widget methods have identical signatures. Pass an explicit empty string for a plain EditBox font. Version 0.1.20 applies this to the macro preview; the API substitute enforces the distinction during page construction. The user's running build was not supplied with the error.

Version 0.1.21 allows explicit automatic weapon placeholders only when readable, complete data produces an inert `#showtooltip` body. Treat placeholders as confirmed owned macros, so existing equipment/bag events can fill them with updates enabled, without another Create or changing their General slot. Do not clear active actions on pending/restricted data. GUI availability and Save share the same rule. The user confirmed main-hand creation works after closing Blizzard's Macros window. Retain that guard: the cached native UPDATE_MACROS handler calls Update and SelectMacro, and SelectMacro replaces MacroFrameText with saved body text. Native textChanged is also set on programmatic SetText, so it alone cannot reliably identify an unsaved user edit. The user's screenshot confirms 0.1.20 macro-page rendering; the running build and native coating execution remain unreported.

An inspect cooldown must schedule the current hover instead of abandoning it. Version 0.1.18 uses one cancellable C_Timer.NewTimer per request, a 0.1-second initial debounce, the existing three-second request spacing, eight-second response timeouts and at most three attempts within 30 seconds. A matching INSPECT_READY allows one second of brief data-read retries before another throttled request; zero, missing or restricted values never enter the cache. Validate current record, GUID and visibility at every continuation. Tooltip clear/hide, feature disable, combat and external NotifyInspect cancel the request and its timer. Request identity prevents old timeouts from cancelling a newer hover. External NotifyInspect takes ownership even for the same GUID; do not clear the client's inspect state or compete with it. PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED can restart a visible hover from its current native tooltip data. The matching Camelot Inspect source warns that flooding requests can drop data, so keep the throttle. Fresh hover behavior and server timing on the running client remain unverified.
